Stats-email body: "fresh stats from the team" → the average result, counting only clients who did the minimum work → the best result as a high anchor → "it's not luck, it's a system" → CTA. Repurpose each study as a static ad, short video, long video, carousel, email and Facebook/LinkedIn posts. ([$720K emails 5:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=307s); [$720K emails 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=92s); [Case studies make millions 19:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1181s))
9. **Long-form case-study ads attract the biggest buyers.** The best long-form ad is a 5–20 minute walkthrough: who the client was, the problem, what changed, the result, ideally confirmed by the client on camera. The Jared case study has taken $1M+ of ad spend. A "cold open case study" (broad result as hook, then how it was delivered) ran nearly $1M over ~2 years without fatiguing. Loom-style 3–10 minute case-study ads have a high cost per call, but prospects arrive "with their credit card out," and most six-figure and enterprise clients came from them. In short ads, keep proof to 1–2 specific, believable named results and save deep proof for the VSL, page and call. ([Content flywheel 7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=466s); [Ads so good 7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=466s); [$1M FB ROAS 9:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=584s))
10. **Proof on the VSL and landing page.** VSL: proof at 30–45 seconds. "Pollute" perception with quick one-line results ("turned off $60K/month in ads", "2K → 200K average views in 90 days", "9,000 leads first week"), then give the *average* result (15.5M views after 3 months), not the best. Proof claims for long copy: 3,000+ clients across 250 industries, 1M+ leads in two years. Landing page: case studies and testimonials below the application; a live Trustpilot feed that includes negatives; a "Show More Proof" button to Clutch/G2; judge changes by revenue per visitor. ([$10M VSL 5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=340s); [$3M funnel 7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=467s); [$3M funnel 12:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=723s))
11. **Proof fixes the objections you never hear.** The deadliest objection ("this looks like a scam") stops people booking or showing, so fix it with the site, testimonials and reminder emails. Contact every booking within 5 minutes and send case studies, testimonials, founder videos and a deck before the call. Cancellations usually come from online reputation (Googling you and finding Reddit), so build a brand-neutral reviews site and keep adding to it until it outranks Reddit. A senior person, not offshore support, answers bad reviews publicly. ([$5M objections 10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=646s); [$10K vs $1M ads 13:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=830s); [9-figure sales teams 36:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2194s))
12. **Flagship, vertical and status proof.** The first client's 100M+ views made him synonymous with his niche, and that flagship study powered early growth. After owning one niche, expand vertically (lawyers, doctors, home care), each with its own case studies. Award trophies to members who reach $1M, $3M and $5M/month and have winners speak at the mastermind; successful members reflect the brand. Early low-price clients exist to produce the case studies that justify raising the price. ([Why I disappeared 3:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KeU8LhMBdbA&t=239s); [Best version overnight 3:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VLNCU_ZM0Gk&t=205s); [$1M/mo offer 26:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=1607s))

> "Your prospects only pay you based on what they believe you can do for them. And case studies are how you prove it." ([Case studies make millions 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=31s))
> "One result told from the right angle can speak to almost anyone." ([Case studies make millions 19:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1150s))
> "Never expect results from someone that has never gotten those results themselves." ([$10M VSL 9:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=588s))

**For G&L:**
- Value buckets: (1) leverage, meaning cash in vs purchase price (e.g. 10% down, 60% seller note); (2) speed, meaning days from first offer to close; (3) cash flow and ROI, meaning EBITDA acquired and payback period; (4) seller outcome, meaning multiple achieved vs first offer, valuation uplift, time to exit, and seller-note income.
- Film three flagship long-form (5–20 min) case studies with the client confirming on Zoom: a corporate professional who bought a trades or services business with ~10% down and a vendor note; a 55+ owner who exited above the opening offer with a seller note funding retirement; a buyer doing a small roll-up. Show structure and timeline, not the full structuring playbook.
- Package every deal four ways: "$2.1M HVAC business, $120K down" / "5.7% cash in" / "4.5× vs a 3× opening offer" / "LOI to close in 90 days."
- Start a #deal-wins channel (LOIs accepted, financing approved, closings, valuation lifts), record intake baselines, and email every closed deal to the whole list with the guarantee in the PS.
- Build a ~30-slide proof deck tagged by problem (no capital, no deal flow, bank declined, no successor, low valuation offer, owner-dependent business). Setters send matching slides before the call, and closers drop them in the moment a pain appears.
- Until client deals exist, lead with Bendik and Josh's own deals and offer founding-client pricing in exchange for written case-study consent built into the engagement letter (anonymize sensitive figures where NDAs require).
- Seed Trustpilot and Google reviews plus a "G&L reviews" page so searches for the brand land on proof. Answer negatives personally, and never give out client contacts.
- Run quick one-line deal results plus an honest *average* outcome (e.g. average cash-in % and time to LOI) in the VSL, with a proof wall below the application.

---

<a id="b-ads"></a>

## B. Paid Ads, Creative, UGC & Organic Content

### B1. Paid ads (Meta): readiness, economics, structure, testing, scaling, benchmarks

**Readiness and unit economics**

1. **Only buy traffic once the offer sells to strangers.** You need revenue, a consistent sales process, settled pricing and a solid offer first. The test: "Can you sell it to a stranger on the street?" Doing well on organic does not mean the offer will work on paid. His organic-converting offer failed on cold traffic, which cost about $500K of debt and 6 months of rebuilding. When ads fail, the cause is almost always the offer. The fastest fix is to model an offer that is already scaling in your industry, because "originality on cold traffic is extremely expensive." ([5 mistakes $30K/mo 11:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=681s)) ([Organic vs paid 3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=184s)) ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=92s))
2. **Use revenue mix as an offer test, and set price for cold traffic.** If about 90% of revenue comes from organic, the offer only works warm. If about 99% comes from paid despite a big organic audience, it sells cold. For a long time they closed about 40% of organic calls and under 10% of paid calls, so price for the paid audience, because that is the revenue you control. Dropping a $3K downsell that sat beside the high-ticket offer raised one client's ROAS about 50%. ([Lessons at $3M 27:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1624s)) ([Lessons at $3M 24:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1471s)) ([Iman Gadzhi call 5:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=342s))
3. **Set KPIs from your own LTV, not from generic benchmarks.** Rule of thumb for services: CAC ≤ 30% of LTV. Worked example: LTV $5,000 → CAC $1,500. At a 20% close rate and 50% show rate you need 20 booked calls for 2 clients, so the target is $150 per booked call. Then adjust for commissions, fees, refunds and rep differences. Work backwards from the client goal to calls, conversations (~200) and leads (~500) to get a monthly budget, and divide by 30 for daily spend. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 35:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2131s)) ([$0–$500K in 90 days 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AZC82IKCiVI&t=0s))
4. **Track ad spend as a % of revenue.** His figures over time: 15% of topline ([$3M in one month 18:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uci8AF7uU9A&t=1115s)), about 20% ([Raw $5M convo 54:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3241s)), and 24% ($700K spend → just under $3M new revenue, about 4× ROAS) ([$700K on FB ads 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=31s)). Competitors at $5–10M/mo run at 30–40%. When spend creeps above 25% of revenue, pull back testing and move budget to proven winners. ([$0→$1M/mo ads 13:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=804s))
5. **ROAS thresholds (from a guest scaling to $10M/mo):** 3× is the goal, 4× is "printing money," 2.5× is OK, 2× means you rely on back-end money, and under 2× is "stealing from the back-end." With about $6.2K new-cash AOV, CPA under $3K gives 2× and under $2K gives 3×. Reps collect 85% of new cash within 30 days. ([Scaling to $10M 9:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=581s))
6. **Benchmarks to sanity-check against:**
   - About $1M of ads produces about $4M of new business ([Buy customers 8:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-sZaKSREzM4&t=527s)), and just over $1M/mo produced $5M+ ([Insane-profit ads 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=61s)).
   - Month one: $120K revenue on about $50K spend, with the founder closing every call ([Every revenue level 7:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=432s)).
   - Spending over $10K/mo puts you in the top 25% of Meta spenders, and below that most businesses waste money ([$0→$1M/mo ads 1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=62s)).
   - High cost per call is fine when deals are large. Their control was about $450 per *qualified* call, and they only talk to about 9% of applicants, so don't compare your CPL with other niches ([Employees made an ad 3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=185s)).
   - Budget several thousand dollars for pixel learning when B2B calls cost $500–1,000 ([Raw $5M convo 1:03:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3790s)).
7. **Never scale spend on a thin or unverified margin, and check which channel actually closes before you do.** Blended attribution hid that about 60% of revenue came from organic. He scaled ads from about $50K to $500K+/mo and was nearly bankrupt within 3 months. Doubling spend from $100K to $200K added almost no revenue, and cutting from $100K to $10K lost only about $20K (one client). The turnaround: cut to about $100/day, double down on what was proven, rebuild the paid funnel. That took him from $80K to $1M/mo in about 4 months. Build a cash reserve before big spend risks. ([$116K/day landing page 3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=184s)) ([Why I disappeared 7:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KeU8LhMBdbA&t=460s)) ([Went broke 22:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1370s))
8. **Once returns are proven, scale on returns, not comfort.** Daily caps of $1K, $5K or $10K are arbitrary ceilings. His media buyer said $5K/day was impossible; the account now runs $50K/day. Lead volume is the easiest lever: you can't double close rate overnight, but you can double leads with a few clicks. Set an explicit risk budget, like a stop-loss. ([Scared of being broke 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=61s)) ([Raw $5M convo 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=30s)) ([1.8% close rate 2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=155s))

**Targeting and account structure**

9. **The creative is the targeting (Andromeda).** Meta reads and transcribes each ad (words, on-screen text, visuals, background) to choose its audience, so interest targeting is mostly obsolete. Name the people your audience follows (e.g. "Hormozi") to reach their fans. Use customer slang: swapping "home service business owner with under five employees" for "one-truck Chuck" cut CPL about 20%. Vague scripts are the most expensive ads, and video beats image partly because it carries more signal. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 14:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=862s)) ([$10K vs $1M ads 16:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=1015s))
10. **Consolidate, and go broad before you niche.** Don't build 50 demographic ad sets; in 2026 the manual control is budget, not audiences. One $1M+/mo client runs exactly two campaigns. Niching early is stock-picking: they started with coaches (mostly broke) and found doctors, lawyers and accountants by going broad. Above about $5K/day you have to broaden anyway (niche plumbers → all home-service owners, US + Canada). ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 33:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2037s)) ([Every revenue level 44:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=2663s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 8:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=494s))
11. **Test in ABO with one ad per ad set, then scale winners in CBO.** In CBO one ad takes about 90% of spend, and ads that CBO ignored sometimes became top performers once given budget. Switching to ABO saved clients 30–50%; one $500K/mo client was wasting 40% of spend. Spend a few thousand dollars testing each ad, then move proven winners into CBO and let Meta scale them. *His view evolved here.* Earlier he said media buying is "easy — allocate budget, Facebook decides," and that since Andromeda creative matters more than structure. Elsewhere he says that at $360K+/mo you "can't just blast it and let Facebook figure it out." ([Manage $1M adspend 4:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=279s)) ([$10K vs $1M ads 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=0s)) ([Raw $5M convo 1:01:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3668s))
12. **Ad-ID trick (has cut costs up to about 20%).** Reuse the same post ID across ad sets and campaigns so likes, comments and shares pool on one post. The copy can't be edited later, so keep guarantees and new deliverables out of evergreen posts. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 46:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2781s))
13. **Protect the account.** Set up a verified business page with a real photo and regular organic posts, 2FA, one personal profile, and two payment methods on auto-pay (call the card issuers to stop declines). Season new accounts by spending the daily cap every day, and put ToS and a privacy policy in the footer. At scale, spread spend across 2–3 ad accounts on the same pixel. A lost account cost one member about $1K/day, so open a new one immediately rather than waiting on recovery. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 54:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=3280s)) ([$700K on FB ads 7:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=436s)) ([5 mistakes $30K/mo 15:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=900s))
14. **Avoid bans.** Check every funnel link before launch. Don't use misleading or income-claim headlines; move result and guarantee claims into the VSL or lower on the page. The ad and page must match. No instant redirects, no fake play buttons, and no "you"-based personal-attribute callouts (especially finance or health). No swearing or deliberate misspellings. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 49:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2969s))
15. **Send the pixel only qualified events.** Lead-gen breaks "trust the algorithm" in three ways: there are few data points ($1K might buy 1–2 qualified calls), the data arrives late (closes come weeks later), and the booked-call event is mostly wrong signal. At a 7% close rate, 93% of reported "wins" weren't buyers. Optimising for booked calls also skews toward people with free time. Route each application outcome to its own thank-you page with its own pixel, gated on revenue, recent marketing spend, LTV and readiness to start now. ([Manage $1M adspend 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=92s)) ([$116K/day landing page 11:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=681s)) ([8 questions 2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=122s))

**Testing protocol**

16. **Test one variable at a time, fastest first.** Order: creative (daily, highest leverage) → landing page (needs several hundred thousand dollars of spend to reach significance) → offer and sales process (months). Within creative, test angles before variations: they burned about $200K on variations of the wrong angle. Fix the top of the funnel first, because halving ad cost is worth far more than a 5% landing-page lift. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 38:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2287s)) ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 32:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1945s)) ([Biggest FB ads mistake 3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YYrQv6IYHhA&t=187s))
17. **Give each test enough money and time.** Budget 3–5× the target KPI per ad ($450–750 at $150/call) and wait at least a week (85% of their prospects book within 4 days); longer sales cycles need longer windows. The math: with a $1K CAC and $30K/mo spread over 120 creatives (about $250 each), validating one batch takes 12–20 months. So make fewer, better creatives or spend more on each. Slow starters can win: the third-place ad in his employee contest nearly got cut in phase 1. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 40:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2441s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=371s)) ([Employees made an ad 21:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1304s))
18. **Read results across windows, never one day.** Use all-time, last 7 days and last 3 days. Before killing a former winner, lower its budget, since ads profitable at $1K/day can break at $2K and recover when pulled back. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 42:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2533s))
19. **Fund testing deliberately, and accept the "failure tax."** Default split: 80% to proven creatives, 20% to testing. With 1,000 creatives, about 900 share the 20% and about 100 winners at or under KPI get the rest. To break a plateau, ring-fence 20–50% for experiments; at about $1M/mo that means $200K a month expected to lose. Real example: his employee contest spent about $76K eliminating losers. About 60% of spend went on ads never reused, 30% of ads booked zero calls, and about 7% of submissions drove most results. ([$3M AMA 8:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=530s)) ([Lessons at $3M 11:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=676s)) ([Employees made an ad 9:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=553s))
20. **Low-budget shortcut: model long-running competitor ads.** On $100/day, open the Meta Ad Library, find the biggest competitors' ads that have run for 6+ months (probably profitable), make about 10 close versions and run them. *Caveat (he says both):* word-for-word clones look like knock-offs to an audience that sees the big brand's ads more often, and they raise costs against a dominant spender. Change enough to reach a different segment. ([Wife Q&A 0:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=34s)) ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 10:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=643s)) ([Message to competitors 1:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=94s))

**Budget ladder, kill rules and scaling**

21. **His per-ad budget ladder:**
    - Launch every new ad at $10/day; first result → $50/day; cost per call under $700 → $100/day; under the $500 account-average cost per call → up to $1,000/day; cap at $3,000/day. Most ads top out around $2K/day.
    - Move one tier at a time and make at most one change per ad per day (a "date last edited" column enforces this). Big jumps destabilise delivery: one ad launched at $10K/day spent only about $2K by midday.
    - To derive your own tiers, give a GPT a 30-day CSV export plus your daily target.

    ([Manage $1M adspend 7:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=465s)) ([Manage $1M adspend 14:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=873s)) ([Manage $1M adspend 9:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=589s))
22. **Kill rules and review cadence.**
    - Turn off any ad set that has spent over $1,000 with zero results, or over $1,000 with only one result. Cut two-result ads that are still too expensive.
    - Daily (about 90 seconds, rolling 3-day lookback): drop anything over KPI by one tier, and don't add budget to single-result ads yet.
    - Weekly: run all-time and 7-day lookbacks for housekeeping.
    - Saved filters: "had delivery," "one result," "scale," "no results." Custom columns: budget, spent, results, cost/result, date last edited. Add colour formatting for the action to take.

    ([Manage $1M adspend 13:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=780s)) ([Manage $1M adspend 12:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=749s)) ([Manage $1M adspend 5:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=342s))
23. **Phased stress test (employee-ad contest).**
    - Phase 1 (48h): 41 ads at $600/day (about $1,200 each); cut ads with fewer than 1 call. Meta spent the whole $51K budget in 2 days.
    - Phase 2: survivors +50% to $900/day (about $3K cumulative); cut ads under 6 calls.
    - Phase 3: $1,350/day (about $7K cumulative), needing 15–16 calls. 11 ads survived as permanent assets, and the top 5 held $237–239 per qualified call (53% below the $450 control) despite +56% spend. Weak ads ballooned to about 3× KPI when scaled.
    - At their spend, an ad that can't scale past about $600/day is of little use.

    ([Employees made an ad 5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=308s)) ([Employees made an ad 8:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=493s)) ([Employees made an ad 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=61s))
24. **"Fatigue" is usually over-concentrated budget.** Pushing one ad too high exhausts the likely converters, Meta then serves poor fits, and lowering its budget can bring it back. One bottom-of-funnel ad (about $2M over 18 months) only works below about 15% of total spend: $1.5K of $10K/day, now about $6K of $40K/day. Keep bottom-of-funnel ads at about 20% of budget; his biggest full-funnel ad takes about 25%. He even says "creative fatigue isn't real": you have exhausted a small audience and need ads that appeal to wider, less-informed ones. ([Manage $1M adspend 8:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=528s)) ([Raw $5M convo 1:03:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3820s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 10:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=649s))
25. **Results follow a power law, so keep hunting for winners.** Three videos took 63% of a $700K month, and one more winner of that calibre is worth about $1M/mo. Earlier, about 13 active ads carried $825K/mo, one at $10K/day and another at $2M+ lifetime. Run many creatives at small budgets rather than hunting one monster ad: narrow $50/day ads can be as profitable as big ones. ([$700K on FB ads 10:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=649s)) ([Employees made an ad 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=31s)) ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 43:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2625s))
26. **Expect diminishing returns.** ROAS becomes logarithmic toward $1M/mo, because only so many interested people are scrolling on a given day. "Recover CAC on the front end" breaks at about $1M/mo spend, so know your true LTV beyond 30 days. At about $1M/mo you also gain omnipresence ("I see your ads every time I open my phone"). Past a certain level, closer capacity rather than ads becomes the cap; closers perform best at 70–80% capacity. ([Organic vs paid 5:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=338s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 17:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=1052s)) ([9-figure sales teams 23:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1425s))
27. **What each spend tier needs.**

| Monthly spend (daily) | New + proven creatives | Angles | Funnels / other |
|---|---|---|---|
| <$10K | mostly wasted | — | — |
| $10–50K ($300–1,670) | 5–10 new + 5–10 running | 1–3 | ONE funnel (one offer → one demographic); job = validate offer on cold |
| $50–180K ($1.6–6K) | 10–15 new + 10–20 proven (newcomers: 100–150/mo) | 3–4 | 1–2; broaden targeting above ~$5K/day |
| $180–300K | 15–30 new + 20–50 proven | ≥5 (aim 10) | 1–2 + low-budget new-funnel tests |
| $360–750K (~$25K/day) | 30–80 new + 30–60 proven | several | multiple funnels + brand faces; daily data analyst |
| $1M+ (~$33K/day) | 70–80+ genuinely new concepts/mo | many | mass-market offer, very wide demo, rising CPA |

([$0→$1M/mo ads 2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=123s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 13:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=835s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 16:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=990s))

**Optimisation signal: ROAS and cost per SQL, not cheap calls**

28. **Optimise budget on ROAS and cost per sales-qualified lead (SQL), not cost per booked call.** *This is an evolution:* his budget ladder keys on cost per booked call, but later videos say cheaper calls are often worse. A campaign that cut cost per qualified call from about $400 to about $100 brought 4× the volume of broke no-shows and lost about $500K–$1M of a week's revenue.
    - **Merge three data sources per ad:** the Meta export (spend, calls), CRM SQLs (rep-confirmed: showed and able to buy), and sales/revenue. You get spend, calls, cost per call, SQLs, cost per SQL, sales, revenue and ROAS per ad.
    - **Move budget by ROAS:** at 9× ROAS raise $3K → $4K/day; at 5×, cut $2K → $1.5K; at 2.7×, cut $1K → $500 or off, even with cheap calls.
    - **Wait for enough sales:** never scale on one sale. At low spend lean on SQL counts (8 SQLs beats 2). An SQL-to-sale ratio of 4:1 is weak.

    ([Insane-profit ads 5:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=307s)) ([$1M ROAS breakdown 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=0s)) ([Top 5 mistakes $1M 9:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=579s))
29. **Attribution and judging by job.** For short-cycle lead gen (about 5 days), optimise on last touch, meaning whatever booked the call cheapest. Blended models are for month-long enterprise cycles. Judge each ad by its funnel role: top-of-funnel on CTR, mid-funnel on trust, bottom-of-funnel on cost per call and ROAS. Keep some lower-ROAS ads running because ROAS isn't their job. ([$3M AMA 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=31s)) ([Raw $5M convo 1:05:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3942s))

**Channels**

30. **Meta suits problem-aware buyers; add channels alongside it, not instead of it.** Meta interruption ads reach problem-aware people before they have looked at competitors. Search reaches solution-aware shoppers who compare 2–3 firms and push on price. When Meta hit diminishing returns at $40K+/day (5× ROAS), TikTok at $3K/day (13× ROAS) was worth adding, with a dedicated hire. Among US households earning $100K+, about 90% use YouTube and about 36% TikTok; YouTube has higher CPMs and higher ROAS. Don't fight a dominant spender on its home platform: use LinkedIn, cold email, long-form YouTube or Google instead. "Touch a prospect in four channels and they think you're the biggest company in the world." ([$100K vs $1M offers 8:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=486s)) ([Raw $5M convo 24:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=1470s)) ([9-figure sales teams 56:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3366s))

> "So much of the success is on the creative and the offer, not the actual media buying." ([$0→$1M/mo ads 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=31s))
> "Out of every 100 wins that we report to Facebook, 93% of them were wrong." ([Manage $1M adspend 3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=216s))
> "Facebook controls the number of calls... But you control the quality of the appointments." ([Insane-profit ads 7:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=430s))

**For G&L:**
- **Calculate your KPI before launch.** Target cost per booked call = CAC cap (≤30% of first-30-day cash per client) × show rate × close rate. *Illustration only:* a $15K engagement, $4.5K CAC, 50% show and 15% close gives about $340 per booked call and about $1–1.7K test budget per ad (3–5× KPI). Do this separately for buy-side and sell-side.
- **Start budget:** enter at Daniel's lowest worthwhile tier, $10–15K/mo (about $350–500/day). Hold back several thousand dollars for pixel learning and a cash reserve so you don't cut winners early. Run one funnel per audience. If cash is tight, launch the side whose offer sells best to strangers and keep the other on organic until the first is profitable.
- **Keep the two audiences in separate funnels and pixels** ("Buy a business" vs "Exit-ready"), because Meta's contextual targeting reads the script. Load buyer ads with their language: "seller financing," "SBA loan," "boring businesses," "corporate job," plus names they follow. Load owner ads with "succession," "no one to hand it to," "EBITDA multiple," "business broker," and their trades (HVAC, accounting practice, distribution).
- **Gate the pixel on qualified applicants only.** Buyers: liquid capital, operating or management experience, a 6–12-month timeline. Owners: revenue/EBITDA band, years owned, planned exit window. Fire the conversion event only from the qualified thank-you page.
- **Stay compliant for both audiences.** Don't write "Are you over 55?" or "Can't get a bank loan?" (personal-attribute callouts). Call out behaviour instead: "If you've run your company 20+ years and none of the kids want it…". Keep "no money down" and deal-return claims out of the ad headline and put them in the VSL.
- **Build the structure:** ABO with one ad per ad set and a ladder scaled to your KPI (e.g. $10 → $50 → $100/day as cost per qualified call beats target). Kill any ad at 3× KPI spend with zero SQLs. Weekly, build the spend → calls → SQL → signed-engagement table and move budget by ROAS, not cheap calls.
- **Plan for long cycles on the sell side.** Owners 55+ likely decide more slowly than Daniel's 4-day bookers, so use 14+-day judgment windows and weight sell-side ads on SQL counts before closes. Add YouTube ads once Meta is profitable, since that is where affluent older owners are.

### B2. Creative: hooks, scripts, angles, formats, volume, testing cadence and the content flywheel

1. **Creative is about 90–95% of the game.** Meta's priorities are user experience, then its own ad revenue (98% of its revenue), then advertiser success. Ads people want to watch get cheaper CPCs and CPLs; clickbait, lazy AI images and poor edits pay a "pollution tax." Set the Super Bowl standard: worth watching even if viewers know it's an ad. Ads now compete with the organic posts above and below them in the feed, so they must feel like content. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=184s)) ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 6:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=399s)) ([Ads so good 1:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=63s))
2. **Clarity first.** A cold stranger must finish the ad knowing what you do, how you help, and why to believe you. At the starting tier, clear communication to the target demographic drives about 95% of front-end success. Make the ad among the highest-quality content the company produces, because it is the first touchpoint. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 9:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=552s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 4:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=247s))
3. **Four-part script: Hook → Mechanism → Proof → one CTA.** Audit every script for all four.
   - **Hook:** call out a behaviour, not a demographic ("If you're struggling to write, film, edit and post all your content by yourself…"). Jump straight to the most interesting part, name the audience precisely ("if your lead cost is over $50"), and pair promise with proof ("500 qualified leads for agents in 30 days").
   - **Mechanism:** use "First, Next, Then," because the #1 cold objection is "I can't picture how this works for me."
   - **Proof:** 1–2 named, specific results.
   - **CTA:** one only ("Click below and I'll show you how to get started").

   ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 19:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1172s)) ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 21:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1264s)) ([$36K 1-1 training 1:35:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=5703s))
4. **Hooks come from sales calls.** Winning hooks were pain stated in prospect language, lifted straight from calls ("Your current agency is charging more, your CAC's getting worse…"). Positioning such as "You hire us, then you fire us" (no endless retainer) closed a $30K paid-in-full deal in week one. Their most important copywriter came from the sales team. Mine headlines, emails and the call pitch for angles that already convert. ([Employees made an ad 15:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=900s)) ([Employees made an ad 16:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=992s)) ([Wife Q&A 2:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=158s))
5. **Match the message to awareness and trust.**
   - *Locus of control:* concepts → things → people → self. Getting it right cut ad costs to ½–⅓ with nearly the same creatives; cold audiences need tactical tips.
   - *Credibility continuum:* observations for cold, experience for warm, expertise for loyal.
   - *Schwartz awareness:* aware audiences (low spend) respond to guarantees, proof and discounts; unaware audiences (high spend) need stories and secrets. Sell the dream, not escape from pain ("How much better would your business be if a million people saw it?").

   ([$36K 1-1 training 43:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=2635s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 9:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=557s)) ([$100K vs $1M offers 10:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=609s))
6. **Angles are different desires or use-cases for the same offer; variations are executions of one angle.** You need 2–10 angles as you scale, and each one extends the offer's life. His 8 angles in one month: guarantee, time savings, new vs legacy media, staffing an editor, views, leads and clients, scaling the business, testimonials. Objections become angles: "ads don't work for me" became "get clients without paid ads," which absorbed several million dollars at strong conversion. Front-end hooks should be "new, better, different"; "slightly better ROAS" sounds like everyone else. ([$700K on FB ads 2:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=156s)) ([$100K vs $1M offers 14:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=884s)) ([$3M AMA 17:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=1026s))
7. **"DNA" framework for generating ads.** Audience (named industries and their language), Talent (who reads it), Goal (clients vs more leads vs better leads), Container (the packaging: e.g. a million views, an autopilot system, a staffed manager). Swap one element at a time to make a new ad. ([$10K vs $1M ads 3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=184s))
8. **Write simply and keep it short.** Aim for a 6th-grade reading level or lower; his biggest videos average 3rd grade, so check with Hemingway Editor. Cut preamble; if the idea fits in one sentence it's a tweet. Write your own scripts (Hormozi writes all his copy). AI is for brainstorming: their "DART" tool turns ICP data into about 60 scripts, and the "Rick" GPT drafts hooks, bodies and captions. ([$36K 1-1 training 1:44:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=6255s)) ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 17:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1079s)) ([64 tools 6:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=372s))
9. **Put about 95% of effort into the video and 5% into copy.** They reused 1–2 headlines and bodies across about $5M of spend, and copy tests never reached significance. Image ads are the exception and need stronger copy; videos also outlast images before fatiguing. Primary text should be short, one sentence per paragraph and at most 1–2 scrolls. Structure it as: problem as prospects voice it → solution → pains to avoid ("not an agency," no lifelong retainer) → one CTA. ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 25:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1510s)) ([2026 Meta Ads Blueprint 27:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=1634s))
10. **Volume and diversity are the real lever.** He gives several numbers:
    - No account can run profitably in 2026 with fewer than about 100 creatives, so get to 100 and then launch. He targets about 800 live ads at $40K/day and 1,000 at $50K/day.
    - Aim for at least one really good video per $5K of spend; less-skilled advertisers need one per $400. A $700K month ran 96 new plus 50 older creatives.
    - Adding 100–300 genuinely diverse creatives cuts CAC 20–40% and often halves costs.
    - Failing accounts run 10–30 ads in one format and message, filmed the same day by the same person. Post-Andromeda, 15 near-identical hook tweaks at $100/day doesn't work.
    - *Contradiction:* he also says that under $1M/mo, creative testing "barely matters — even slop/AI ads convert," and his tier table asks only 5–10 new creatives/month at $10–50K. Read the 100-creative floor as his newer (2026) position.

    ([$3M AMA 7:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=438s)) ([$700K on FB ads 1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=62s)) ([Learn this 5:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=307s)) ([Every revenue level 21:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=1275s))
11. **Run a format mix, because each format does a different job** (from his $1M ROAS breakdown):
    - **Founder talking heads:** highest ROAS, with a higher cost per call but lower cost per SQL. If sales complains about lead quality, run more of these.
    - **Long-form case studies (Loom-style, 3–20 min):** expensive per call, but prospects arrive "with their credit card out," and most six-figure and enterprise clients came from them. One cold-open case study ran about $1M over about 2 years without fatiguing.
    - **B-roll + text loops:** look organic and work for top of funnel and retargeting, but don't build trust alone.
    - **Statics and carousels:** cheapest call ($424) but poor cost per SQL and ROAS.
    - **AI-generated ads:** tested worse than statics.

    Turning off B-roll dropped account-wide ROAS. Keep every format running, with smaller budgets on the weaker ones. ([$1M ROAS breakdown 6:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=368s)) ([$1M ROAS breakdown 7:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=430s)) ([Ads so good 7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=466s))
12. **Statics: mixed advice.** He calls static text-on-image the least efficient, most expensive lead-gen format, and says image-only accounts probably won't be profitable now. Yet image ads can beat video in a niche where everyone runs video. Meta also feeds each user the formats they consume, so always run a mix. Ranking real ROAS by format (case-study ads beat statics) improved marketing efficiency 50–60%. ([Biggest FB ads mistake 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YYrQv6IYHhA&t=31s)) ([Ads so good 6:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=373s)) ([Focus on revenue 3:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_oZlUE9Eygg&t=214s))
13. **Proven formats to copy:**
    - (a) Short looping iPhone video with carefully worded on-screen text: about $250K in 60 days, made in under 5 minutes.
    - (b) B-roll loops with *enough* copy (guarantee + how it works + CTA): took 1st and 3rd in the contest. Earlier text reels failed because they had too little copy. Use real ICP-congruent footage, such as someone walking down a street; walls of text over stock footage fail.
    - (c) Podcast-style clips: about half the cost of the next-best ad. Use clips, not full episodes.
    - (d) Short bottom-of-funnel offer video (bold offer → how it works → click): about $250K in 2 months.
    - (e) Cold-open case study.

    Long videos raise average view duration (30–60s on a 10-minute video), which makes retargeting cheaper. Always look for formats competitors in your niche aren't using, and borrow from unrelated industries. ([Ads so good 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=0s)) ([Employees made an ad 24:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1461s)) ([Content flywheel 5:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=344s))
14. **Edit lightly.** In a 2025 split test across about 100 videos, minimal editing (A-roll, plain Arial text, no music or effects) beat heavy editing both organically and in paid. Over-produced ads hid the message. Feel personal, "like talking to a friend," even in B2B. Get the basics right: decent lighting, eye contact, a clean background, on-screen hook text and captions (70–80% watch without sound). An aspirational setting (suit, premium office) works if the talking points still come through. ([Lessons at $3M 30:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1839s)) ([Employees made an ad 20:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1212s)) ([$36K 1-1 training 1:39:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=5981s))
15. **The content flywheel (continuum).** The formats run: short text → long text → short loop with on-screen text → short talking head → long talking head/case study → podcast → training. Start where you're strong and push every piece into 2–3 other formats. Short formats are fast but face more competition and build less trust; long ones cost more and build trust. Scale a proven winner through every format: "a million views or you don't pay" became about 100 variants (vertical, horizontal, loop, carousel, static), and one winner became dozens in each format. ([Content flywheel 1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=93s)) ([$10K vs $1M ads 5:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=338s)) ([Content flywheel 16:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=989s))
16. **Zero-budget quick starts.**
    - Write the offer in one sentence in the phone notes app, screenshot it and run it as a 9:16 ad (10 in 5 minutes). Website headlines and FAQs are ready-made copy.
    - Paste winning emails as notes screenshots. For long text, put a hook on the image and point people to the caption, or split the paragraphs into a carousel. Longer on-screen text is harder to copy.
    - Shoot 5-second clips (office, walk, coffee) and overlay existing ad text.
    - Read existing primary copy from a teleprompter as a 15–30s talking head: 10 ads become 30.
    - Turn each case study into a static, short video, long video, carousel, email and LinkedIn post.

    ([Content flywheel 3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=187s)) ([Biggest FB ads mistake 2:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YYrQv6IYHhA&t=125s)) ([Case studies 19:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1181s))
17. **Make ads funnel-specific and congruent.** Each ad should know its funnel, offer part, page and action; for example, guarantee ads go to a guarantee funnel and story ads to a "scale" funnel. Make dedicated top-, middle- and bottom-of-funnel ads rather than making every video full-funnel. Prospects forget the ad 10 seconds after becoming a lead, so the funnel has to carry the conversion. ([$0→$1M/mo ads 11:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=710s)) ([Ads so good 8:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=526s)) ([Ads so good 9:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=587s))
18. **Run creative like a production line.**
    - Name every ad with: ad # per creator, creator, abbreviated hook, length, format, and MMYY scripted. Example: "Ad160_Daniel_10M views or you don't pay_1:12_vertical_0425," which spent about $325K in a month at 3.27× ROAS. Track names in Airtable.
    - Use Frame.io for review.
    - Track creatives, new creatives/mo, angles, funnels and faces per dollar of spend.
    - At $1–1.5M/mo they keep 100–200 creatives in backlog via an in-house copywriter, designer and junior buyers. Big lead-gen buyers test about 1,000 ads per vertical per week.

    ([$700K on FB ads 8:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=497s)) ([$0→$1M/mo ads 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=0s)) ([I failed 18:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=1087s))

> "You should always be looking for a format of creative that is underutilized by other advertisers." ([Ads so good 0:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=32s))
> "If you don't have more than 100 ad creatives in your ad library, you are essentially doing nothing." ([Wife Q&A 1:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=64s))
> "Creative diversity is what makes the whole thing work." ([$1M ROAS breakdown 8:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=492s))

**For G&L:**
- **Build a 2×5 angle matrix before filming.**
  - *Buyers:* "the seller is the bank" (vendor finance), "quit your job by buying cash flow, not starting from zero," "boomers are retiring with no successor," "why searchers overpay — and how to structure 10% down," "you don't need to be rich, you need a deal structure."
  - *Owners 55+:* "what your business is really worth vs what you think," "no successor? here's your exit in 24 months," "get paid more by selling on terms," "the 3 things buyers discount you for," "don't let a broker list you unprepared."

  Test angles before hook variations.
- **Script with First/Next/Then.**
  - Buyer: "First we find off-market owners with no successor. Next we structure the deal so the seller finances most of it. Then you take over a business that already makes money."
  - Owner: "First we score your exit-readiness. Next we fix what buyers discount. Then we run a competitive sale."
- **Take hooks from your recorded calls.** Pull the 20 most-repeated phrases, e.g. "The bank wants 30% down and I don't have it" or "My accountant says it's worth 3×, the only offer was 2×," and use them verbatim.
- **Use contextual keywords.** In buyer scripts, name the acquisition-entrepreneurship voices and terms that audience follows ("SBA 7(a)," "ETA," "search fund," "boring business"). In owner scripts, name trades and situations (dental practice, HVAC, accounting firm, "kids don't want it").
- **Reach 100 creatives within the first 60 days.** About 15 founder talking heads (2 angles × buyer/owner), 20 notes-app statics of VSL headlines and FAQs, 20 teleprompter reads of the best emails, 15 B-roll loops of real footage (site walk of a client's acquired business, signing at a lawyer's office) with guarantee + how-it-works text, 10 podcast clips (an advisor interviewing a retired owner who sold on terms), and 3–5 long Loom deal walkthroughs. A walkthrough covers price, deposit, vendor-note terms and post-close results, anonymised as needed. Expect these to bring your highest-intent sell-side leads.
- **Measure ROAS by format, not cost per call.** Budget more toward founder talking heads and case studies if SQL quality matters most. Keep statics small and use B-roll loops as retargeting.
- **Adopt the naming convention from day one** (e.g. "Ad012_Founder_SellerIsTheBank_0:58_vertical_1026") and keep a backlog of at least 20 ready creatives. Aim for about one strong new video per $5K of monthly spend.

### B3. UGC, brand faces and employee-made ads

1. **Why add faces.** People buy from people who look and talk like them, and each demographic needs its own spokesperson: a 45-year-old plumber and a 25-year-old entrepreneur respond to different presenters. Hiring about a dozen UGC creators to read one proven script was "one of the highest-leverage moves in company history." Adding female creators cut costs almost 30%. Above about $100K/mo spend, UGC is a no-brainer. ([$10K vs $1M ads 7:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=461s)) ([$10K vs $1M ads 4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=246s)) ([Learn this 6:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=400s))
2. **Prerequisite: a proven control script.** Read the script on camera yourself and prove it with Meta spend first; otherwise you can't tell whether a flop was the script or the actor. Ideally the ad already returns 4–8× ROAS at launch. Then test one variable at a time: proven scripts on new creators, then new scripts on a proven creator. New talent reading proven scripts skips most testing spend. ([UGC system 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y0SVI--8iDI&t=30s)) ([UGC system 6:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y0SVI--8iDI&t=367s)) ([$1M ROAS breakdown 9:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=584s))
3. **Economics.** Creators cost $25–100 per video; his system runs about $30 per video plus about $5 for subtitles, so about $35 per creative and about 500 UGC ads a month. 100 ads cost a few thousand dollars, and one creator can turn around up to 100 a day. ([UGC system 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y0SVI--8iDI&t=0s)) ([Learn this 6:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=400s))
4. **Recruiting and running creators.**
   - **Volume:** start with at least 10 creators (it's a numbers game). 50 creators × 5–15 videos gives a large pre-validated bank.
   - **Trial:** a paid trial of 5 videos (5–15) at $30 via Backstage. Most can shoot five in a day, and anyone who doesn't deliver the same day probably isn't a long-term fit. Keep imperfect takes for variety, and offer winners long-term work at a higher rate on bigger batches.
   - **Job post:** name the company, say scripts are provided and editing is in-house (creators only film and send raw files), require good production quality, state the $30 trial pay, and link the ICP brief.
   - **Brief:** delivery instructions, avatar and buying motivation, who you are, the parts of the offer that sell, why your company, and payment terms. Sell yourself as a stable long-term partner.
   - **Remove guesswork:** send example videos (ideally your own), a publicly openable script doc and a Drive upload link.

   ([UGC system 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y0SVI--8iDI&t=92s)) ([UGC system 3:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y0SVI--8iDI&t=214s)) ([UGC system 4:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y0SVI--8iDI&t=275s))
5. **Manage brand faces as a portfolio.** He tested about 16 brand faces with great success. Add 1–2 new faces a month, and when a face fatigues (costs rise), rotate in a new spokesperson for part of the budget. Split budget by face: Daniel about 75% → 65%, Margot about 20%, Nico about 3% (cut for weaker reception), Josh ramping toward 20%. Key-person risk proved smaller than feared: new faces with their own pages matched or beat the founder's CPA. ([$0→$1M/mo ads 15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=928s)) ([$700K on FB ads 5:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=311s)) ([Scaling to $10M 12:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=733s))
6. **Solve the "VSL problem."** Ad-to-page congruence matters. A creator's ad leading into an unknown founder's VSL converts worse ("who's this guy?"), and a female presenter's ad into a male presenter's VSL caused a big drop. There are two fixes:
   - The creator records their own VSL and pre-call video. Margot's own VSL failed because she didn't know sales or the product as well.
   - Use a neutral no-VSL page: headline plus application. This now takes about 80% of traffic and is the unlock for 16, or even 1,600, faces.
   - **Current routing:** founder ads → VSL; employee and creator ads → application.
   - **Rollout path (guest):** test a new face to the main funnel → their own landing page with photo → their own VSL.

   ([VSLs are dead 4:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=244s)) ([Scaling to $10M 33:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1984s)) ([Raw $5M convo 1:08:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4096s))
7. **Know the quality trade-off.** UGC actor talking heads bring easy calls but a higher cost per SQL. Some prospects booked because they liked an attractive actor and were let down by the rep. About $750K went into UGC in 90 days, mostly testing, which pulled ROAS down; founder talking heads still have the highest ROAS. A guest's coach-made and client-testimonial UGC programmes failed through poor management (no feedback loop, no follow-through), not because of the creators. If the founder is the face but doesn't deliver, sell "my method," never "I'll personally coach you." ([$1M ROAS breakdown 8:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=523s)) ([Scaling to $10M 29:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1740s)) ([$3M AMA 2:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=158s))
8. **Employee-made ads: the internal contest.**
   - **Prizes and timeline:** about $8.5K pool ($5K / $2.5K / $1K) and about $50K test budget. Announce 2 weeks out, raw files due 1 week out, the team edits, then a 7-day test; winners announced day 8.
   - **Protocol:** same copy, headline, funnel (no VSL) and starting budget for every ad, each run to about $2,100. KPI = cost per *qualified* call against the prior 30-day control (about $450).
   - **Results:** 11 permanent assets with the top 5 at about $237/call, 53% below control (see B1 #23).
   - **Why it worked:** sales reps and account managers know prospects' language; without that, expect about a tenth of the result. Second place was the creator's first-ever video, and plain stripped-down pitches work ("12 million views — that's what our average client gets after 3 months").
   - A guest ran the same play with 20+ staff and prizes for the top three.

   ([Employees made an ad 2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=155s)) ([Employees made an ad 10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=646s)) ([Employees made an ad 22:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=1367s))
9. **Staff on-camera roles.** At $1M+/mo he has 2 full-time on-camera employees, contractor talent and dedicated designers, with 300+ active ads and a goal of 1,000. A sales rep with hundreds of thousands of followers failed at selling ("you're too far ahead") and now produces dozens of ad creatives a month as a brand rep. The in-house marketing team (13 people) includes UGC creators, platform-specific buyers and a script/film/edit crew. ([Harsh lessons 6:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=368s)) ([Lessons at $3M 1:12:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4347s)) ([100-person team 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=92s))

> "With ads, the failure tax is what you have to pay to buy confidence." ([Employees made an ad 9:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=584s))

**For G&L:**
- **Run a G&L ad contest first, because your advisors and closers already speak the language.** Each films 2–3 ads in their own words from real calls. Use a scaled-down pool (e.g. $1.5K / $750 / $250), the same headline, copy and application page for all, and each ad run to 3–5× your qualified-call KPI.
- **Match faces to audiences.** For exit ads, use a 55–65-year-old advisor or former owner who sold, since owners trust peers who have done it. For buyer ads, use a 30–45-year-old operator who acquired with vendor finance. Test a female face on both early (his ~30% cost drop).
- **Bring in UGC only after a founder-read control script is proven** (ideally 4×+ ROAS). Then trial 10 creators × 5 videos at about $30–35 each, all reading that script, with editing done in-house.
- **Creators speak as spokespeople ("here's how G&L works"), never as fake clients.** Daniel's own data shows attractive-actor UGC brings easy calls that disappoint on the call, and trust-heavy 55+ sellers will check. Use real client clips (owners who exited, buyers who closed) for testimonial ads, and run a #client-wins channel so material gets captured while the engagement is live.
- **Route by face.** Founder ads → founder VSL; advisor and creator ads → a neutral text page + application (or their own short page with photo). Never send an advisor's ad into a VSL of someone the viewer hasn't seen.
- **Split budget by face** (start with the founder at about 70–75%) and add 1–2 faces a month once spend passes about $50–100K/mo.

### B4. Organic content vs paid: how they work together

1. **Run both ("2 + 2 = 10").**
   - Organic gets you in front of new audiences for free, and you then retarget them cheaply. Ad viewers in turn get fed your organic posts, which builds trust faster.
   - Because organic covers the top and middle of the funnel, paid can go almost entirely to bottom-of-funnel and retargeting. Retargeted organic finders convert much better than cold traffic.
   - Organic reach also makes ads cheaper. In months with 10M+ organic views his ROAS improves about 5–10% (on about $800K/mo of Meta spend), and pausing long-form YouTube for about 10 days made Meta costs jump.
   - If organic works on a platform (e.g. LinkedIn), add paid on that same platform first.

   ([Organic vs paid 10:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=614s)) ([5 mistakes $30K/mo 26:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1584s)) ([9-figure sales teams 53:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3181s)) ([Roasted 6-figure owner 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=31s))
2. **Use organic as a free creative lab, with limits.**
   - Most of his most profitable ads started as top organic posts: take the hook and body, add a CTA, run it. Companies spend 20–50% of ad budget on testing, and skipping that can halve costs. A bad hook fails organically too.
   - Middle-of-funnel posts test angles: for a plumbing coach, "make money online" flopped while "get home for dinner, stop taking night calls" won.
   - *Limits:* a top-of-funnel post built for views can have a great CTR and still not convert. In a Dave Ramsey example, the video with 10× the views had far lower ROAS. Judge each piece by its job.

   ([Content flywheel 11:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=711s)) ([Organic vs paid 11:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=674s)) ([$36K 1-1 training 1:02:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=3722s)) ([Lessons at $3M 34:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2054s))
3. **Which comes first.** With little money, start organic: it's free and the worst case is lost time. With revenue, a sales process and a cold-proof offer, paid buys predictable volume. One member got 5–15 quality applications a day from ads versus about 1 a day organically. For high ticket, ads are more predictable because every ad is a direct bottom-of-funnel pitch, and "if you had to double next month, it would come from paid." Organic-only means a lean, high-margin business that depends on content; paid is harder but bigger (he went from 2–3 staff to about 100). Choose deliberately. ([Organic vs paid 1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=91s)) ([5 mistakes $30K/mo 12:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=742s)) ([Organic vs paid 8:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=522s))
4. **Track the two channels separately, because their economics differ sharply.**
   - Organic leads convert about 3× better than paid and are easier to fulfil, while cold-traffic clients are far less forgiving.
   - At about $500K/mo, organic produced 5–10% of leads but up to 60% of revenue. At another point organic was 40–60% of revenue, a $150–300K/mo zero-CAC base. Now about 80% of sales volume comes from ads and organic brings 20–40% of revenue; referrals are under 1% and outbound under 0.01%.
   - Build identical but separate funnels per source. One six-figure client turned out to have never seen an ad.

   ([$116K/day landing page 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=153s)) ([Focus on revenue 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_oZlUE9Eygg&t=30s)) ([$3M funnel 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=0s)) ([Went broke 10:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=622s))
5. **For high ticket, optimise for buyers, not views.**
   - The goal is money, and what matters is who the views come from. Deliberately exclude beginners and make longer, more sophisticated content for owners who can pay. Technical videos got about 1/10 of the views but about 100× the link clicks.
   - View spikes don't add applicants (he reads them as an inverse signal), and content-sourced buyers convert 3–6 or even 6–10 months later. Niche services need a few views from the right people.
   - Don't dumb content down. In-depth, unpolished analysis ("just talk about what you do," Loom-style) attracts senior owners and A-level hires.

   ([Why I don't get views 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=153s)) ([9-figure sales teams 42:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2562s)) ([$1M/mo offer 14:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=866s)) ([Iman Gadzhi call 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=92s))
6. **Formats and platforms.**
   - **Short-form talking head** is the default start: a 1-hour interview yields 20–30 shorts, about a month of content. Film dedicated vertical rather than cutting from long-form (one creator's numbers rose about 47% in 60 days), and keep short-form consistent with your long-form depth.
   - **Long-form YouTube** is expensive (tens of thousands a month; they ring-fenced 2 years of salaries) but builds deep trust and recruits talent. After 3 months they had 6,000 followers plus podcast and collab connections he used to pay $18–24K for.
   - **Podcasts:** no organic distribution without an existing audience, but 31 episodes puts you in the top 5%, and "I saw your podcast" is close to a guaranteed sale. Mock podcasts built for clipping beat clipping real ones. A 1,000-view reel has brought $300–400K/mo deals.
   - **Long trainings** (1.5–2h) only after short clips on those topics prove demand.

   ([$36K 1-1 training 19:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=1181s)) ([Lessons at $3M 52:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3123s)) ([9-figure sales teams 50:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3057s)) ([Content flywheel 10:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=650s))
7. **Quality × quantity, then reuse.** Result = quality × quantity. One client cut output to a third, spent 3× longer scripting, and gained 20× per video (13 → 200+ followers and 100K+ views per post). Then repost winners on a schedule: one video got 3K views, then 100K and 100K on reposts, and he got 10–100M views/mo during 18 months without filming. Recycle winning concepts for sub-audiences: an Amex black-card video got 4M views, and 10 follow-ups brought 400K followers in 30 days. Mine competitors' comments for ideas, and let comment quality pick your pillars. ([$36K 1-1 training 7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=466s)) ([$36K 1-1 training 1:22:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=4960s)) ([5 mistakes $30K/mo 6:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=404s))
8. **Funnel mix, asks, and DMs.**
   - From zero: about 80% top-of-funnel and no asks for the first couple of months. Established service brands posting about 60×/mo: about 20% top and 80% middle/bottom.
   - Every ask spends brand equity, so keep public posts "give" and sell in private: a ManyChat keyword → DM with lead magnet + founder voice note + calendar link.
   - Response-video hooks: "Someone just asked me…", "I get this DM three times a week…".
   - Small accounts should be narrow and tactical, not broad and profound.

   ([$36K 1-1 training 33:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=1987s)) ([$1M business for a stranger 17:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=1052s)) ([$36K 1-1 training 1:17:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=4648s))
9. **Organic does credibility work for paid leads.** Sophisticated buyers check your profile after booking. Use your personal name everywhere, a work-setting photo, a 150-character bio (what, for whom, benefit), and a link to the apply page with a short VSL, not the homepage. Short-cycle buyers decide in 48–72 hours, so don't hype a product weeks ahead; pack the persuasion into the window after the lead arrives. Emails that link catalogue YouTube videos took about 5 minutes each to write and made $144K in 6 months, while also building retargeting audiences. ([5 mistakes $30K/mo 24:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1459s)) ([$36K 1-1 training 2:26:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=8819s)) ([Emails $720K 4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=245s))
10. **Leverage and persistence.** Paid plus organic social brings thousands of calls a month; a competitor estimated he'd need about 50 outbound reps to match. Founder social is "the most important thing a founder can do": he batches meetings into one day a week and spends tens of thousands a month on social payroll. Expect it to feel weird until it works (his first ~30 videos were hidden), allow 6–12 months on camera and about 200 failed posts, and "do something in real life" before posting about it. ([Hustle culture 1:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ppgr1dhJzAM&t=63s)) ([Why I disappeared 13:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KeU8LhMBdbA&t=834s)) ([Scaling to $10M 1:03:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3799s))

> "Organic lets you get your foot in the door with a new audience completely for free, and then just retarget that audience with ads." ([Organic vs paid 10:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=644s))
> "40 to 60% of all of the company revenue came from organic social media. The videos that cost me nothing to post." ([Why I disappeared 7:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KeU8LhMBdbA&t=460s))
> "Find out what works for free, then invest money in it and let Facebook do the rest." ([Content flywheel 12:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xvOnEA48xZY&t=743s))

**For G&L:**
- **Run two founder-led organic series:**
  - *"Deal Breakdowns"* for buyers: a 10–20-minute YouTube walkthrough of one real or anonymised deal (price, % down, vendor-note terms, cash flow after debt service), cut into 5–10 vertical shorts.
  - *"Exit Ready"* for owners: valuation myths, what buyers discount, timelines. Aim it at the 55+ owner on YouTube and Facebook, where that demographic is.
- **Make response videos to the comments you get most:** "Can you really buy a business with no money?", "Why would a seller finance the deal?", "My business is worth more than that." These double as angle tests: anything that beats your organic median gets a CTA and goes into paid.
- **Purify the audience.** Skip "get rich buying businesses" hype. Go technical (deal structure, earn-outs, working-capital pegs) to attract qualified buyers and serious owners. Track link clicks to the apply page, not views.
- **Advisors post on LinkedIn about 3×/week** with deal specifics; train someone in-house rather than using a ghostwriter. If LinkedIn organic pulls owners, add LinkedIn paid there before trying a new platform.
- **Retarget organic viewers** (YouTube and Instagram video viewers, page engagers) with bottom-of-funnel ads: a case-study ad and "book your exit-readiness review / acquisition strategy call."
- **Set up ManyChat keywords** (e.g. "DEAL" → seller-finance term-sheet example; "EXIT" → exit-readiness checklist) followed by a founder voice note and calendar link. Point every profile link at the apply page with the VSL.
- **Build separate funnels and UTMs for organic vs paid from day one,** and compare close rates. If organic closes far better, fix the cold offer before raising spend.

---

<a id="c-funnel"></a>

## C. The Funnel: Architecture, VSL, Landing Page, Email, Application, Setting & Show-Up

### C1. Funnel architecture

1. **The base flow.** Traffic (paid or organic) → application page with a direct offer and no opt-in gate → application with friction and routing → booking page → thank-you/pre-call page that sets expectations → pre-call reminder and qualification sequence → sales call (discovery + close). Before the call the prospect should already have seen content and ads, applied, been warmed and qualified by a setter, had several reminders and seen proof again and again, so the call opens with some trust. ([$116K/day landing page 1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=91s), [Close $1M/mo as a rep 1:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=94s))
2. **The funnel after the click does the converting.** Most prospects forget your ad 10 seconds after they become a lead. ([Ads so good 9:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=htaJ8CkoWSk&t=587s))
3. **Choose calls or webinars from the unit economics, then build everything around that choice.** One-to-one calls convert better, so you need fewer leads, can pay more per lead and can sell a higher ticket. Webinars need more, cheaper leads and support a lower ticket. At $10–25K he would use 1:1 calls. ([5 mistakes $30k/mo 21:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1304s))
4. **Fit the funnel to the real sales cycle.** In his B2B business most leads decide within 72 hours and the cycle is about 5 days, and 85% of prospects book within 4 days. Put the persuasion into the 48–72 hours after a lead arrives, not into weeks of pre-launch hype. ([5 mistakes $30k/mo 22:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1367s), [Meta Ads Blueprint 40:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2441s), [$3M AMA 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=31s))
5. **Run one funnel until it is proven.** He reached $1.4M/month on one funnel, one product, one price, one delivery method and one customer type. At $10–50K/month ad spend, run one funnel (one offer to one demographic). At $50–180K, run 1–2. At $180–300K, run 1–2 plus low-budget tests of new funnels. ([Raw $5m/mo 1:09:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4187s), [Ads $0 to $1M/mo 2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=123s))
6. **Above roughly $100K/month, build a funnel for each angle and each presenter.** The ad's angle, offer, page and VSL must match. Every guarantee-angle ad goes to a guarantee funnel, and "scale your business" story ads go to a scale funnel. Viral Coach ran five funnels: million-view guarantee, Daniel-fronted, Nico-fronted, Margot-fronted, and no-VSL. When a female presenter's ad led to a male presenter's VSL, conversion dropped sharply. Angle-specific funnels also widen the buyer pool past the $100–200K or $1M/month ceiling. ([Ads $0 to $1M/mo 11:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=710s), [$700K on FB ads 4:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=281s), [$3M funnel 0:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=33s))
7. **Roll out a new brand face in three steps.** First, test their ads into the main founder funnel and measure CPC, cost per call and sales. Next, give them a landing page with their own photo. Last, give them their own VSL. All funnels merge at the application. Key-person risk turned out smaller than feared: new faces matched the founder's CPA and sometimes beat it. ([Scaling to $10M 31:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1862s), [Scaling to $10M 12:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=733s))
8. **His view on multiple funnels changed.** After cutting CPA about 60%, the $10M team tested five funnels: 3–4 VSL opt-in variants, a direct application and a low-ticket funnel. Results were mixed and similar, so the theory that everything feeds one "vortex" was false. Separately, adding a no-VSL funnel took Daniel from $1.4M to $3M the next month. ([Scaling to $10M 11:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=672s), [Raw $5m/mo 1:09:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4187s))
9. **Build a funnel for each buyer type, and keep it short for sophisticated buyers.** For example: "If you're a big business and want me to make ads, click here", followed by a short form. Buyers who already know what they want need less information. Long educational funnels attract the "tell me what I need" buyer you don't want. ([$1M business for a stranger 5:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=341s), [$1M business for a stranger 7:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=436s))
10. **Build separate, otherwise identical funnels for each traffic source** (Meta, Google, IG organic, YouTube organic, email) so you can see lead quality and revenue by source. Organic leads converted about 3× better than paid. Blending sources once hid that about 60% of revenue came from organic. He then scaled ads 10× and came close to personal bankruptcy. ([$116K/day landing page 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=153s), [$116K/day landing page 3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=184s), [Focus on revenue 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_oZlUE9Eygg&t=30s))
11. **Give each routing outcome its own thank-you page and pixel.** Then only qualified leads send Meta a positive signal. Meta otherwise optimises for the cheapest leads, which are mostly the worst. In Viral Coach's case 93% of booked-call "wins" sent to Meta were not buyers. ([$116K/day landing page 11:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=681s), [$1m/mo adspend 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=92s))
12. **Sales capacity has to lead marketing spend.** When prospects see no open slots they leave, CAC rises and spend has to be cut. Aim for about 80% calendar utilisation. Closers do best at 70–80% of capacity, and past a point extra ad spend produced no extra revenue because closers were the bottleneck. The $10M team's target was a steady 150 calls booked in every 48-hour window, keeping calendars about 90% full. ([I failed 8:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=497s), [9-figure sales teams 19:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1175s), [Scaling to $10M 3:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=182s))
13. **Optimise from the top of the funnel down:** ad → landing page → application → booked call → show → qualified → closed. Halving ad cost matters far more than a 5% landing-page lift. Tune the middle and bottom once the data is significant. Test in this order: creative (daily), then landing page (needs several hundred thousand dollars of spend for significance), then sales process and offer (months). ([Biggest FB ads mistake 3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YYrQv6IYHhA&t=187s), [Meta Ads Blueprint 38:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2287s))
14. **Judge the funnel on revenue, not on cheap leads.** Use cost per SQL and ROAS rather than CTR, opt-ins or cost per booked call. One campaign cut cost per qualified call from $400 to $100 but quadrupled the volume of broke leads who didn't show, and it lost a week of revenue (about $500K–$1M). ([$10k vs $1m ads 10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=646s), [Top 5 mistakes at $1M 9:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=579s), [Ads for profit 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=61s))
15. **Benchmarks.**
    - KPI math: CAC at or below 30% of LTV. With LTV $5K, CAC is $1.5K; at 50% show and 20% close that means 20 booked calls per 2 clients, so about $150 per booked call.
    - Viral Coach today: about $450 per booked call, about $1,500 per qualified call that shows, and about 15% of those close.
    - Monthly flow at $1M+ spend: 2,671 leads, about 39% DQ'd, 1,633 live calls, about 48% show.
    - Close rate by definition: 17% qualified-show-to-close, 12% show-to-close, 8% confirmed-call-to-close, 6.6% call-to-close, 1.8% lead-to-close.
    
    ([Meta Ads Blueprint 35:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2131s), [Lessons to $3M 35:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2115s), [$5M/mo sales system 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=31s), [1.8% close rate 8:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=530s))
16. **Fix the objections you never hear.** This is survivorship bias, like the WWII planes. The people who think "this looks like a scam" never book or never show. Fix the website, testimonials and reminder emails for them. Cancellations usually come from online reputation (prospects Google you and find Reddit). Answer that with a brand-neutral review site such as "xyzreviews.com" and keep adding to it until it outranks Reddit. ([$5M/mo objections 10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=646s), [9-figure sales teams 36:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2194s))
17. **Never sell to unqualified leads. Route them somewhere useful instead.** Free training, a free Skool group (courses, AI tools, gear lists), or a low-ticket community or downsell priced 5–10× below the core. When a qualified prospect says "I want to buy", give them a way to pay on the spot, not another case study. ([$3M funnel 21:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1305s), [Organic vs paid 11:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Hk0k66Pc5Dk&t=705s), [Roasted 6-figure owner 9:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=561s))
18. **Ascension needs a bridge.** Sending $50 product buyers straight into a $7K VSL application failed because they had no warm-up. Low-ticket front ends ($50–$2,000) work only with one built-in ascension path, such as a qualifier call then a closer call (80–90% show rates). ([Scaling to $10M 23:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1406s), [Scaling to $10M 13:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=795s))
19. **Keep the content CTA simple.** Offer a free business breakdown at a short, memorable one-word URL, and point the profile link to the apply page with a short VSL, not the homepage. ([Landing page doesn't convert 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8_oYHxgy-yA&t=215s), [$36K 1-1 training 2:26:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=8819s))

> "Facebook controls the number of calls... But you control the quality of the appointments." ([Ads for profit 7:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=430s))
> "under no circumstances do we sell our services to those who are not qualified to work with us" ([$3M funnel 22:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1336s))

**For G&L:**
- Run two completely separate funnels: **Buyer** ("acquire a profitable business with little money down using seller finance") and **Seller 55+** ("get exit-ready and sell for a premium"). Each needs its own ads, page, application, thank-you pixel and calendar. Don't merge them until each is proven.
- Measure your real decision window before copying the 72-hour rule. Sellers deciding on an exit will likely take longer than Viral Coach's B2B buyers. Track days from application to booking and from booking to close, and extend kill windows and nurture to match.
- Fire the Meta pixel only on qualified thank-you pages. For buyers that means capital/liquidity at or above your minimum and a timeline under 90 days. For sellers it means revenue or EBITDA above your floor and a sale horizon under 24 months.
- Model capacity before you spend. Calls per advisor per week × show rate × close rate sets the maximum ad budget. Keep advisors at 70–80% booked.
