- Route non-fits instead of dropping them. Under-capitalised buyers go to a free "first deal" training or a low-ticket community. Sellers 3+ years out go to an exit-readiness checklist plus long nurture.
- Use separate tracking URLs and funnels for organic, YouTube and paid so you can see which source produces closed retainers, not just booked calls.
- Build a review and case-study hub that ranks for "G&L M&A Advisory reviews", so cautious 55+ owners who Google you find proof rather than a forum thread.

### C2. VSLs: structure, length, and the "VSLs are dead" view

1. **What a VSL is for.** It borrows trust from a founder for a product whose outcome is unclear or conditional, such as coaching, consulting or marketing. High-trust purchases with clear outcomes (realtor, accountant, lawyer, a $50K car) sell without one, and so do offers where before/afters say more (landscaping, cosmetic). ([VSLs are dead 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=30s), [$116K/day landing page 6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=371s))
2. **How his view changed (the "VSLs are dead" arc).**
    - He started with a VSL because a $20K marketer told him to, and it got him to $1.4M/month.
    - He later said to test without a VSL first, because a good one is hard to make.
    - Now about 80% of traffic goes from a creator's ad straight to a headline-plus-application page or short text page with no VSL. That converts best, and he says "the most profitable VSL is no VSL."
    - His reasons: buyers have seen so many VSLs that the format itself signals "this needs a pitch to make sense"; and many UGC faces can't each record a 10-minute VSL.
    - He hedges: VSLs still work (some companies do $10M+/month with them), he still uses them for founder-face ads, and he suspects turning the VSL off would lower overall ROAS through delayed conversions. He suggests testing that with geographic holdouts.
    - He also says the $4M/month came from fixing the offer, not from the roughly 20% lift from removing the VSL.
    
    ([Raw $5m/mo 1:09:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4187s), [$10M VSL 11:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=711s), [VSLs are dead 2:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=152s), [VSLs are dead 3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=183s), [Raw $5m/mo 1:08:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4096s), [VSLs are dead 5:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=305s))
3. **If you depend on a VSL, the real problem is an offer that can't be stated simply.** Fix the offer so it makes sense to people who don't already trust you. Building an offer that converts without a VSL is also what lets dozens of UGC creators make ads. ([VSLs are dead 4:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=275s), [Ads $0 to $1M/mo 15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=928s), [$700K on FB ads 6:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=374s))
4. **The person in the ad must be the person in the VSL.** A founder-on-camera ad flows into a founder VSL. Text or UGC ads that land on an unknown founder's VSL lose viewers ("who's this guy?"). A creator presenting her own VSL also failed because she didn't know sales or the product well enough. Current routing: founder ads go to the VSL, and creator or text ads go to a neutral text page or straight to the application. ([VSLs are dead 3:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=213s), [Scaling to $10M 33:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1984s), [Raw $5m/mo 1:08:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4096s))
5. **Timing and length.**
    - 0–15s: what you do and the benefit.
    - 15–30s: why it beats what they've tried.
    - 30–45s: proof and testimonials.
    - 45–60s: recap and CTA.
    - Then 3–10 more minutes repeating, word for word, the lines that get the best responses on sales calls.
    - From social traffic, attention drops at around 2.5–3 minutes, so put the critical information in that window. Then add about 2 minutes for skeptics: budget, authority, need and timing objections, value reinforcement, and comparison with alternatives.
    - Expect the same 3–5 seconds of attention as the ad gets.
    
    ([$116K/day landing page 7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=433s), [$116K/day landing page 8:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=493s), [Landing page doesn't convert 3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8_oYHxgy-yA&t=184s))
6. **Every section delivers one of the three Ps: Promise, Proof or Plan** ("we can do this specifically for you, here's how"), pitched at different awareness levels. The hook puts promise and proof in one line, ideally through the guarantee ("a million views or you don't pay"). Add a visual open loop ("1 million views = how much revenue?"). Structure it like a YouTube video: hook, then the promise, then proof ("I've made 50 others X, here's how you're the 51st"), then get to the point. ([$10M VSL 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=0s), [$10M VSL 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=30s), [Landing page doesn't convert 2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8_oYHxgy-yA&t=154s))
7. **Give the recipe early.** Avoid the "recipe blog" problem of opening top-of-funnel and reaching the details after 20 minutes. ([$10M VSL 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=92s))
8. **Say who you can't help, specifically and more than once.** It makes ideal prospects feel the offer was built for them. ([$10M VSL 2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=124s))
9. **Call out the audience in detail** and name problems only they have had (e.g. big spend on traditional marketing for a few hundred site visits). Then restate why you're different. ([$10M VSL 4:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=280s))
10. **In the Plan section, lead with the single strongest reason your mechanism beats the alternatives.** Make "how it works" a run of objection answers: custom strategy (answers "cookie-cutter?"), what am I paying for, done for you so it costs little time (answers "no time"), and everything else gets easier (answers "worth it?"). If the guarantee sits on an intermediate metric, tie that metric to revenue straight away. ([$10M VSL 3:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=187s), [$10M VSL 7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=433s), [$10M VSL 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=61s))
11. **Go through proof quickly.** Use one-line results ("turned off $60K/month in ads", "9,000 leads first week"), then the *average* result, not the best. ([$10M VSL 5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=340s))
12. **Take every line from sales calls that made money, and log why you lose calls.** Rebuild the VSL to answer the most common loss reasons. ([$10M VSL 3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=218s), [$10M VSL 8:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=495s))
13. **Persuasion devices he uses on purpose:**
    - appeal to authority (follower count, big-brand clients)
    - a dig at providers who never got results themselves
    - circular proof ("this works because it got you here")
    - pre-empting price ("not the cheapest, but the best ROI")
    - push-away/pull-in
    
    ([$10M VSL 8:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=525s))
14. **End with one CTA.** Make the most direct pitch, then: "short application below to see if you qualify; we'll map out the process on a call." Point non-fits to free training so the step feels like there's nothing to lose. Put the pitch in a VSL with the form underneath, not in a Typeform that has to act as the sales page. ([$10M VSL 10:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=618s), [Roasted 6-figure owner 7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=467s))
15. **Production.**
    - Design the thumbnail like a YouTube thumbnail that shows the questions the video answers (who it's for, how it works, why it works, next steps).
    - Production quality can stay low.
    - Host the video on Vidalytics to see watch time and drop-off.
    - Viral Coach offers a free custom GPT that drafts VSLs.
    
    ([Landing page doesn't convert 2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8_oYHxgy-yA&t=123s), [$116K/day landing page 18:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=1084s), [64 tools 16:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=985s))

> "Every line in here was once said on a sales call that made us money. None of this is speculative." ([$10M VSL 4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=248s))
> "People created VSLs to fill this trust gap" ([VSLs are dead 2:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=152s))

**For G&L:**
- Start by testing whether you need a VSL at all. M&A advisory sits nearer the clear-outcome professional-service end (realtor, accountant) than coaching. Split-test a short text page against a founder VSL, and break results out by ad type.
- Buyer VSL hook (promise and proof in one line): e.g. "We help you acquire a profitable business with 0–10% down using seller finance. Here's the last deal we structured." Open loop: "How does the seller get paid if you don't have the cash?"
- Seller VSL hook: e.g. "Get your business exit-ready and sold, without leaving money on the table." Open loop: "What is your business actually worth to a buyer today?"
- Say who it's not for, more than once. For buyers: "not for people looking for a passive side hustle or with no operating experience or partner." For sellers: "not for owners who want out in 30 days at any price."
- Build "How it works" from objection answers. Buyers: "Why would a seller finance me?", "Where do deals come from?", "What if it fails?" Sellers: "Will buyers see my numbers?", "Will my staff find out?", "Vendor finance means I don't get paid?"
- Use only lines that have closed deals on your calls. Log every lost call's BANT reason and re-cut the VSL every quarter around the top two.
- The first 2.5–3 minutes must cover what you do, why it's different, proof (one or two anonymised deals) and the CTA. Add a 2-minute skeptics' section on fees, timeline and the risk of the seller-finance note.
- If you add a second presenter, such as a partner fronting the seller funnel, give them their own page and VSL. Don't send their ads into the founder's video.

### C3. Landing pages

1. **The headline matters most.** The first few words on the page are the most important words in the business. Their job is to get everyone to take the next (qualifying) step, not to sell or to disqualify. Put the whole offer in one short line, built around the single thing that sells best right now, even if you offer a dozen services. Examples: "1 million views guaranteed" for cold traffic, or "Grow your business with social media" for more aware prospects. Multi-sentence, number-heavy headlines are too much. ([Landing page doesn't convert 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8_oYHxgy-yA&t=0s), [$116K/day landing page 5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=340s), [Landing page doesn't convert 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8_oYHxgy-yA&t=92s))
2. **Meta traffic is interruption traffic: visitors weren't looking for you.** Too much text, too many numbers, a slow load or an unclear offer sends them back to scrolling. 99% of traffic is mobile, so build for mobile and near-instant load, and check page speed first. A 1-point gain at the top compounds all the way down. ([Landing page doesn't convert 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8_oYHxgy-yA&t=30s), [$3M funnel 4:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=249s), [$10k vs $1m ads 13:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=799s))
3. **Page order (tested over hundreds of iterations):**
    - brand name/logo
    - a quick reputation plug (Trustpilot)
    - a short, angle-specific headline or description
    - an optional angle-specific VSL
    - more proof
    - a "See if you qualify"/apply button that jumps to the application, which must be visible above the fold
    - the application
    - then case studies and testimonials for skeptics
    
    Hormozi workshops, Contrarian Thinking and Hyros use the same structure. ([$116K/day landing page 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=215s), [$3M funnel 4:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=249s))
4. **Below the application, stack proof:**
    - a headline case study (e.g. a client who turned off $60K/month in ads)
    - dozens of testimonials across industries
    - a live Trustpilot feed, including negative reviews, so skeptics can do their own due diligence
    - a "Show More Proof" button that leads to case studies, Clutch and G2; savvy B2B prospects respond well to being sent off-page for more proof
    
    ([$3M funnel 7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=467s), [$3M funnel 12:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=723s))
5. **The no-VSL text page.** A short text page explaining what you do now takes about 80% of Viral Coach traffic, and diverse ads do most of the selling. A neutral text page fits every UGC creator's ad. ([$10M VSL 11:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=711s), [VSLs are dead 3:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=213s), [VSLs are dead 4:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AATxUdXv4tI&t=244s))
6. **His view on design reversed.** In the $116K/day video he says simple sells: his VSL isn't highly produced, friends doing $3–10M/month use near-blank white pages, and the page that converts wins. Later he says the 2020-style page (white background, headline, VSL, application) now signals an unprofessional business. A professional redesign doubled lead conversion overnight and held for 30 days. Reconcile the two by testing a few variations and letting data decide. ([$116K/day landing page 18:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=1084s), [$10k vs $1m ads 12:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=738s))
7. **Judge page changes by revenue per website visitor**, which captures both conversion rate and brand trust. Landing-page tests need several hundred thousand dollars of spend to reach significance. ([$3M funnel 12:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=723s), [Meta Ads Blueprint 38:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2287s))
8. **Keep the ad and the page consistent.** Meta bans mismatches. Keep income or guarantee claims out of the ad headline and put them in the VSL or lower on the page. No instant redirects and no fake play buttons. Check every funnel link before launch. ([Meta Ads Blueprint 49:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2969s))
9. **The thank-you page carries a short prep video** that sets expectations for the call, plus more testimonials. The calendar shows slots about 3 days out with a timezone confirmation. ([$3M funnel 13:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=814s))
10. **Copying a page won't copy the results.** Competitors copy Viral Coach's ads, pages and case-study design word for word. The moat is the machine behind them: data, reps, fulfilment, contract structure. ([Competitors 0:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=32s), [Scared of being broke 6:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=368s))

> "The first few words that you have on your landing page, they're really the most important words in your business." ([Landing page doesn't convert 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8_oYHxgy-yA&t=0s))
> "It's not the fanciest page that wins. It's the one that converts the best." ([$116K/day landing page 18:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=1114s))

**For G&L:**
- Buyer page headline test set (one line each): "Buy a profitable business with little money down", "Own a $1–5M business using seller finance", "Acquire a cash-flowing business without risking your savings". Keep claims compliant: put deal numbers lower on the page, not in the ad headline.
- Seller page headline test set: "Sell your business for what it's worth, on your timeline", "Get exit-ready in 6–12 months", "What would a buyer pay for your business today?"
- Build each page in this order: G&L logo → review stars or "X deals closed / $Y transaction value" → headline → optional 3-minute video → "See if you qualify" → application above the fold on mobile → deal tombstones (anonymised: industry, price, % down, % seller-financed) → testimonials from buyers and exited owners → "See more deals" link to a case-study hub.
- Sellers 55+ are likely to judge on professionalism. Test a polished advisory-firm design against a bare page rather than assuming the 2020 white-page template works.
- The thank-you page should hold a 60–90 second advisor video: "here's what we'll cover, have your capital and financing picture ready" for buyers, and "have last 3 years' P&L and your rough target number" for sellers. Add 3 testimonials.
- Track revenue per visitor by page and ad type, not opt-in rate.

### C4. Email (including the $720K emails)

1. **How much email matters, and when to build it.**
    - Email has stayed at 2–3% of top-line revenue at every stage, and under 5% in another account (about $150K of a $3M month from about 50K active readers).
    - Conversion is about 0.02% even on proven content.
    - Until about $1M/month, set up only call reminders and cart-abandon emails, then think about long nurture. For most small businesses the extra 5% isn't worth heavy sequence work.
    - This sits uneasily with the "$720K emails" video, where five emails made over $500K in about 6 months. Read it as: worth doing cheaply by reusing proven assets, not worth building from scratch early.
    
    ([5 business lessons 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=0s), [Raw $5m/mo 1:07:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4034s), [$720K emails 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=0s))
2. **Subject lines are your tested headlines.** The first email's subject is the highest-converting landing-page headline ("1 million views or you don't pay"), so the prospect sees the proven words a third time after the ad and the page. The second- and third-best headlines become the next subjects ("great results with very little time"). ([$720K emails 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=0s), [$720K emails 1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=60s))
3. **The "fresh stats" body template:**
    1. "fresh stats from the team this morning"
    2. the average client result, counting only clients who did the minimum work (e.g. 15+ videos over 3 months)
    3. the best client result, as a high anchor that makes the average feel achievable
    4. common thoughts business owners have
    5. "it's not luck, it's a system"
    6. what the system looks like
    7. CTA
    
    ([$720K emails 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=92s))
4. **Never forget the PS.** Everyone reads it, so restate the guarantee or best headline there ("if it doesn't work, you don't pay"). Use the PPS for a wall of reviews. ([$720K emails 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=153s))
5. **Every incentive that works on calls belongs in email too.** A 7-day trial raised call revenue 30%, and the trial email became one of the best performers. ([$720K emails 3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=183s), [$720K emails 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=215s))
6. **The YouTube-link email:** a short paragraph and a link to an existing video. About 5 minutes to write and $144K in 6 months, and it also grows YouTube and builds retargeting audiences. They now send one catalogue video a week. ([$720K emails 4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=245s))
7. **Send every client win to the whole list.** Case-study emails were the 5th to 9th most profitable. Format: before (344 followers) → after 90 days (100K followers, 10M views) → screenshots → "click here to work with us" → PS with the guarantee. ([$720K emails 5:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=307s))
8. **Repurpose emails from top social posts,** for example a 100K-view short. Case studies also get turned into emails. ([Raw $5m/mo 1:07:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4034s), [Case studies 19:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1181s))
9. **Long-term nurture for non-bookers and lost leads:** wait 24 hours, then send 22 emails over 45 days. Response fell off sharply after 45 days. Build the emails from the highest-converting content and best parts of the programme, written by the founder and CMO. Run SMS/email drips and social retargeting alongside SDR calls to leads more than a month old. ([$3M funnel 20:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1245s), [100,000 sales calls 1:30:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5400s))
10. **Sell inside owned channels.** Skool lets you email all members about every 72 hours. Do the bottom-of-funnel selling there and keep the public ask to "join the free group." ([$1M business for a stranger 34:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=2062s))
11. **For pre-call reminder emails** (timing, channel alternation, open/reply as a quality signal), see C7.

> "Take your landing page copy, as long as you know it works, make it an email, and make more money because of it." ([$720K emails 1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=60s))
> "Never forget the PS." ([$720K emails 6:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=369s))

**For G&L:**
- Use the top three tested page headlines as subjects for nurture emails 1–3 in each funnel. Repeat the winner in every PS, plus a wall of buyer and seller testimonials in the PPS.
- Buyer "fresh stats" email: average deposit as % of price, average seller-finance %, average days from engagement to LOI, counting only clients who reviewed X deals or submitted Y LOIs. Anchor with the best deal.
- Seller "fresh stats" email: average multiple achieved against the initial valuation, average months exit-prep to close, and the best uplift as the anchor.
- Case-study email for every closed deal. Buyer example: "Bought a $2.1M HVAC business with $120K down + 60% seller finance". Seller example: "65-year-old owner exits at 4.5× after 9 months of prep". Include redacted LOI or term-sheet screenshots where permitted.
- Every week, send one existing YouTube or VSL-segment video, such as "How a vendor-finance note works" or "5 things that kill your sale price".
- If a low-friction entry offer works on calls (a paid deal review or valuation snapshot), put it in an email too.
- Nurture: 22 emails over 45 days for buyers. For sellers, consider a longer, lighter monthly cadence after day 45, since their timelines run in quarters, but test this rather than assume it.

### C5. Application / qualification forms and lead scoring

1. **What the application does:** qualify financially and pre-frame so nobody is surprised by the price on the call (e.g. "$30K per video"). Include timeline checks ("~1 month turnaround, does that fit?"). Write down the ideal client's traits so the team can vet for them. The two biggest qualifiers are an income/revenue threshold and the type of offer the prospect runs; finer points build up over time. ([$1M business for a stranger 6:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eWMz7IfiHbE&t=373s), [$1m/mo offer 18:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=1114s))
2. **The current Viral Coach fields:**
    - first name, last name
    - business email
    - phone
    - "Tell us about your business" (open text)
    - monthly revenue brackets
    - last-30-days or historic marketing spend
    - years in business
    - when they plan to start
    
    Put a dollar value (revenue per call) on every answer. ([8 questions 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=31s), [$3M funnel 5:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=310s))
3. **Collect contact details first** so drop-offs can still be called, emailed and retargeted. Typeform's partial-response capture needs a paid plan (about $100/month). ([Roasted 6-figure owner 10:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=622s))
4. **Scoring signals and their measured effect:**
    - Email domain: custom-domain business emails earn far more per call than Gmail, Yahoo or Hotmail. Route common-domain leads to weaker reps; this gave about 250% better throughput.
    - Phone area code: Australian leads beat US and UK leads, a 278% difference. Prioritise them without shutting the others off, since UK leads still bring about $83K/month. Area codes also reveal time zones.
    - Time in business: under 1 year is worth far less than 1–3 years (248%).
    
    ([8 questions 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=61s), [8 questions 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=153s), [8 questions 11:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=682s))
5. **The open-text answer is a filter.** Scan it for disqualifying keywords (for him: D2C jewelry/cosmetics, auto shops, salons, clothing, real-estate agents) and judge its length. One to three words means low quality; a paragraph means invested. This removed 12% of leads, and reps may decline calls. ([8 questions 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=215s))
6. **Ask about past budget, not future budget.** "What have you spent trying to solve this?" and "last-30-days spend" get honest answers because past spend predicts future spend. For B2C, get at ability to pay indirectly (e.g. a dating offer asks how much they spend on clothes). ([8 questions 7:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=432s), [$3M funnel 6:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=403s))
7. **Score answers in combination.** Under $10K revenue with under $1K spend produced basically zero revenue and is always declined. That bracket showed 53% show, 43% qualified and 3% close, about $115K across 179 calls, which wasn't worth the team's time. Exceptions include founders with a big prior exit. Low budget with good revenue is often someone avoiding an upsell, so keep them. ([8 questions 8:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=495s), [8 questions 4:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=277s))
8. **The start-date question is useful but not enough.** Anyone 30+ days out is DQ'd and sent to free training. That's only about 8% of leads, but it creates written timing commitment that reps confirm at the start of the call. "Ready to start now?" with only "now/within a week" allowed to book removes junk, but it's a knee-jerk answer prospects forget, so the call still has to handle timing. ([8 questions 12:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=743s), [Timing objections 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=0s), [8 questions 13:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=806s))
9. **Use wide brackets on purpose** (e.g. $50–100K) so the setter or closer has a reason to ask "closer to 50 or 100?". People answer more exactly in conversation. Setters double-check answers: one "$1M/month" applicant was a kid quoting potential revenue. ([$3M funnel 7:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=437s), [100,000 sales calls 9:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=554s))
10. **There are two levers: friction and routing.** Qualifying questions raise cost per lead but improve quality, because people willing to disclose are less skeptical. Halving the discovery questions lifted total applicants about 30%, so keep the form quick, not a 20-minute process. Keep 2–3 question levels and switch weekly with rep capacity: level 3 (strictest) when reps are full, level 1 ("send in the hordes") when volume drops. Script the questions word for word, and use ChatGPT to tighten timing and budget questions. ([$116K/day landing page 9:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=589s), [Roasted 6-figure owner 7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=467s), [9-figure sales teams 19:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1144s))
11. **Route by score to tiered booking pages.**
    - Revenue example: under $10K/month → free training; $10–50K → reps with more time per lead; $50K+ → most experienced reps; $250K+ → flagged for special treatment.
    - Scores send leads to tier-1 closers, mid-tier, third-tier, or SDR/DQ.
    - Sending the best leads to the best reps took revenue per lead from $500 to $1,000 overnight and doubled the business. "Just browsing, not deciding within 30 days" goes to the weakest reps.
    
    ([$116K/day landing page 10:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=620s), [$3M funnel 2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=155s), [9-figure sales teams 32:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1951s))
12. **Disqualify with questions, not copy.** Don't write "this is for you if you spend $100K/month." Ask the question (under or over $100K) and quietly route non-fits to free content or a low-ticket downsell, so you still monetise the rest of the audience. ([Roasted 6-figure owner 5:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=344s))
13. **Deliberate friction for a premium offer.** One example: a name/email opt-in that isn't even saved, a "do you read?" question, and no self-scheduling (the team reaches out instead), all aimed at getting more of the wrong people to say no. He still makes room for impressive applicants when full, and turns away technically qualified but weak or high-maintenance ones. ([$1m/mo offer 20:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=1208s), [$1m/mo offer 35:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=2129s))
14. **Junk filters and pickiness.** Discard applications submitted between 11pm and 3am; one operator found 57% of completed applications were fake. More volume lets you be pickier: about 5,000 applications produced only about 1,300 calls (about 70% DQ'd), and one business talks to just about 9% of applicants. Before scaling into cheaper leads, make sure the filtering can protect the calendar. ([9-figure sales teams 34:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2073s), [1.8% close rate 3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=186s), [Top 5 mistakes at $1M 10:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=610s))
15. **At scale, answers predict lead value before the call.** With thousands of applications behind you, answers alone predict what a lead is worth. Use colour-coded tiers with their own averages and KPI reps against their tier. ([Harsh business lessons 4:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=276s), [Every number tracked 14:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=862s))
16. **Should closers see the answers? Views conflict.** Daniel has setters dig into vague answers ("you said real estate: brokerage, agent or course?") so closers bring matching case studies, and his reps confirm each answer at the start of the call. Matt (9-figure sales conversation) hides answers from closers because their bias becomes self-fulfilling. Daniel's reps wrongly avoided real-estate leads that had closed five times that month. ([$3M funnel 18:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1120s), [9-figure sales teams 33:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2012s))

> "When we started giving the best leads to our best reps, our efficiency went from $500 to $1,000 per lead overnight." ([$3M funnel 3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=218s))

**For G&L:**
- **Buyer application (in this order):**
    1. First and last name, email, mobile (captured first)
    2. "Tell us about your background and what kind of business you want to buy" (open text: score length; flag "passive income", "no experience", "dropshipping")
    3. Liquid capital available today, in wide brackets: <$25K / $25–100K / $100–250K / $250K–1M / $1M+
    4. "What have you already spent on courses, advisors or deal costs?" (past spend, not budget)
    5. Target business size, by EBITDA or price bracket
    6. Industry experience or operating role (years managing P&L or people)
    7. Whether a partner or spouse is involved in the decision
    8. "If accepted, when would you start actively making offers?" (now / <30 days / 30–90 / 90+; send 90+ to training)
- **Seller application:**
    1. Name, business email, phone first
    2. "Tell us about your business" (industry, what it does)
    3. Annual revenue and EBITDA/owner earnings in wide brackets
    4. Years owned
    5. Owner age band (55–60 / 60–65 / 65+)
    6. Ideal exit timeline (<12 months / 1–2 years / 2–5 years / just exploring)
    7. Owner dependence ("Could the business run 4 weeks without you?")
    8. Other shareholders or spouse in the decision
    9. Whether they've had a valuation or a broker before (past spend and seriousness)
- **Scoring:** a business-domain email beats Gmail, especially for sellers. Area code tells you time zone and region. Buyer score = capital × timeline × experience. Seller score = EBITDA × timeline × owner-dependence (lower is better).
- **Tiered routing:** sellers with $1M+ EBITDA and a <24-month timeline go straight to the senior advisor. Buyers with $100K+ capital and a start under 30 days go to top closers. Everyone else goes to junior advisors or a setter. Under-capitalised buyers and sellers 5+ years out go to free training or nurture, and their pixel doesn't fire.
- Don't put "for buyers with $250K+" in the copy. Ask the question and route quietly, and offer a low-ticket "deal-finder" community as a downsell.
- Keep two or three question levels ready and switch weekly based on advisor capacity. Discard 11pm–3am submissions only after checking your own data, since some owners do apply late.
- Have the setter clarify bracket answers ("closer to $250K or $1M?"). Have the advisor confirm the start date and capital in writing at the top of the call.

### C6. Appointment setting

1. **Contact every booking within 5 minutes on three channels: SMS, email and a real phone call.** Interruption-based cold traffic books cold. Send the meeting link and pre-call material: case studies, testimonials, proof of work, founder videos, a pitch deck, relevant YouTube videos. Contacting within 5 minutes made a sale 100× more likely (HubSpot/MIT). Leads reach the dialer by API about 0.1 seconds after opt-in, so setters must stay logged in. Measure setters on quality hours online (6–7 per day), not on calls made. ([$10k vs $1m ads 13:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=830s), [100,000 sales calls 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=153s), [9-figure sales teams 13:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=833s))
2. **Rep-intro texts.** Four minutes after the instant confirmation email, three automated iMessages go out "from" the assigned rep ("Hey, this is Jared… can you send me your social links?"). They include the prep video as a YouTube link, which lifted views about 25% compared with the page alone. An earlier version: confirmation email with link, plus an intro text from the rep asking one qualifying question. Reps reply to engaged leads with thanks and a screenshot of the prospect's website, to show they've done their homework. ([$3M funnel 14:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=875s), [$116K/day landing page 14:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=865s), [100,000 sales calls 4:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=278s))
3. **What setters do:** they're "glorified receptionists" doing speed to lead. They call within minutes of booking to check qualification, offer an earlier slot, gather notes for the closer, send relevant proof, and dig into vague answers so the closer brings matching case studies. Soft opener: "Hi, I just got your application, took a quick look at your business, I really like what you do, I had a couple of questions so we're better prepared." ([100,000 sales calls 7:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=432s), [100,000 sales calls 7:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=462s), [$3M funnel 18:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1120s))
4. **Setter BANT on a 5–15 minute call:**
    - Authority: "do you run it yourself or have a team?" and "who else needs to be on this call?"
    - Need: "what's not working / what's the strategy?", moving from goals to quantified problems
    - Budget, asked indirectly: "how do you get leads? Cost per lead? How many a month?"
    - Timing: "if this goes well, when would you implement?"
    
    Triage calls should take 7–10 minutes; longer calls showed no better results. Track calls ended under 15 seconds (fix the opener) and under 2 minutes (the move from opener to problem). ([100,000 sales calls 10:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=617s), [Roasted 6-figure owner 3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=186s), [9-figure sales teams 17:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1051s))
5. **Watch for over-qualification.** 80% of 10-minute setter calls ended on a budget objection, but budget came up in only 20% of 60-minute closer calls. Asking "can you afford our minimum package?" before showing value burned leads and left closers only the near-certain deals. Check that objection frequency on setter calls matches closer calls. ([100,000 sales calls 13:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=800s))
6. **Chase applicants who don't book.** About 20% finish the application but don't book. If they haven't booked within 5 minutes, an SDR texts and double-dials within 5 minutes, tries again later that day, then once every 24 hours for 3 days, then moves them to long-term nurture. Cancels and no-shows get daily contact for 5 days to rebook, then are removed or nurtured. ([$3M funnel 18:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1090s), [$3M funnel 18:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1120s))
7. **Assign leads to SDRs by seniority.** New SDRs work aged leads, then move up to cancels and no-shows. Only top performers get the "receptionist" seat on fresh inbound, which is the highest value and highest risk. After a month, SDRs call every paid lead that didn't convert, which doubles as a training ground. Failing closers can drop to SDR (the top SDR made $24K commission in a month). ([100-person team 5:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=343s), [100,000 sales calls 1:30:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5400s), [$20K/mo rep playbook 14:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=867s))
8. **When to hire setters: his statements conflict.**
    - Early on he ran 15-minute setting calls and 30–45-minute closing calls himself. He says the best next hire for a still-selling CEO is usually an SDR, and his own first sales hire was an SDR/assistant for his calendar, DQs and pre-call messages.
    - At $700K/year, a founder taking discovery calls is a low-skill use of time, so hire a $60–80K setter.
    - In the "$3M funnel" video he says they reached $3M/month with closers only, didn't need setters before about $3M/month, and are only now piloting them.
    - Today: about 20 closers and 24 SDRs, added alternately depending on the bottleneck.
    
    ([100-person team 4:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=249s), [Roasted 6-figure owner 3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=186s), [$3M funnel 17:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1057s))
9. **Professionalise the role.** Rename "setter" to client-support or admin coordinator. Hire law students and professional-services admin staff as W2 employees with KPIs to confirm calls and run 30-minute Zoom calls. They outperform teenage setters selling to millionaires. ([Scaling to $10M 55:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3307s))
10. **Build compliance into the system and staff for when leads arrive.** Entry-level setters miss legal exposure (e.g. three calls at 9pm in the prospect's time zone). Many opt-ins arrive 6–8pm while teams finish at 5pm, so staff evenings. One client stopped calling at school pickup and reached a 91% contact rate. Schedule changes alone lifted revenue about 30%. ([9-figure sales teams 8:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=495s), [9-figure sales teams 15:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=926s))
11. **Setting benchmarks.**
    - Contact rate: 60%+ on dialing alone, about 80% with SMS and email added (best seen: 92%).
    - Unique dial rate: 30–40% is ideal; 10–15% means you need more leads.
    - Cadence: about 3 calls a day, at most about 10 a week.
    - Too many leads drags contact rate down. At 7th Level, 50K leads/month with only about 8% reached; Matt would have cut lead flow 60% and fixed speed to lead.
    
    ([9-figure sales teams 12:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=740s), [9-figure sales teams 10:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=648s), [9-figure sales teams 1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=62s))
12. **DM setting for organic.** A ManyChat comment keyword triggers a DM that needs a button or reply. Then send the lead magnet and a founder voice memo. Voice-note script: thanks → a qualifying question about their goals → "regardless, I'm down to chat, what's your week look like?" + calendar link → "let me know when it's booked, notifications get weird." ([$36K 1-1 training 2:23:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=8634s))

**For G&L:**
- Within 5 minutes of a booking: confirmation email, then three iMessages from the named advisor, then a call from a coordinator. Buyer text: "Hi [Name], it's [Advisor] from G&L. Saw you're looking at [industry] businesses. Quick one: are you buying solo or with a partner?" Seller text: "Hi [Name], [Advisor] here. Looking forward to talking about [Business]. Is anyone else (spouse, partner, shareholder) who should join us?"
- Use a soft coordinator opener and a 7–10 minute triage. For buyers: capital source, decision-makers, deal criteria, start timing. For sellers: who else owns or decides, why now (health, retirement, burnout), and whether recent financials are ready.
- Don't ask "can you afford our fee?" in triage. Leave fee and value to the advisor, and check that setter-call objections match closer-call objections.
- Hire coordinators from law, accounting or brokerage admin backgrounds and call them "client coordinators". 55+ owners will respond better to professionals than to young setters.
- Apply-but-didn't-book chase: within 5 minutes, again the same day, then daily for 3 days, then nurture. No-shows and cancels: daily for 5 days.
- Staff calls in early evening and lunchtime for employed buyers, and business hours for owners. Enforce calling-hour rules by time zone in the dialer.

### C7. Show-up rates & pre-call nurture

1. **Show-rate benchmarks.**
    - Viral Coach team: about 48% on cold traffic. Another breakdown: about 40% no-show.
    - Passive reps on tier-3 leads: about 30%. Top reps double that by texting, emailing and calling before the call and following up fast on no-shows.
    - The top rep reached 84% (team 48%) by talking to prospects beforehand, with a slightly below-average DQ rate (34% vs 39%). His messages are shared for others to copy.
    - Pre-call follow-up can double a rep's commission.
    
    ([$5M/mo sales system 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=31s), [Sales onboarding 1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=91s), [$5M/mo sales system 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=153s), [Every number tracked 7:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=461s))
2. **The booking window: his advice changed.**
    - Early advice: book 2–7 days out (occasionally 8–9), never weeks. B2C needs shorter windows, and home services should call back within the hour.
    - Data from "8 questions": calls booked 1–6 hours out averaged only $49 per call because there's no time to warm the prospect. 6–72 hours is much better, and 72–96+ hours can be worth more still (a 737% difference). So don't restrict windows the way gurus advise.
    - The biggest show-up factor is how soon the call is, since most people plan no more than 4–5 workdays ahead.
    - The live calendar shows slots about 3 days out.
    - Synthesis: aim for roughly 1–4 days, with enough time to nurture and not so long that the prospect forgets.
    
    ([$116K/day landing page 13:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=804s), [8 questions 15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=928s), [Raw $5m/mo 44:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2662s))
3. **Reminders alternate channels and always include the link. Versions differ.**
    - (a) Email 24h before, text 3h, email 1h, text 30 minutes.
    - (b) Email 24h before (only if booked further out than that), SMS 4h, a channel switch at 1h.
    - (c) Email 24h, SMS 4h, email 1h, SMS 30 minutes, then a final SMS at 5 minutes: "the meeting has started."
    
    Elsewhere he warns against texting someone whose call is 30 minutes away, or who booked at 2am, because it lowers show-up. Start simple; it grows to 100+ automations at scale. ([$116K/day landing page 15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=928s), [$3M funnel 15:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=935s), [100,000 sales calls 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=153s))
4. **Don't over-nurture affluent buyers.** Sending 5–8 emails in 24 hours reduced interest. One week of testing a heavy pre-call contact system popular on YouTube cost about $300K. Heavy nurture suits lower-income B2C buyers (fitness, dating) and annoys multi-million-dollar owners. Only $100K+ enterprise deals should get fewer touches. Once a lead confirms, ease off and drop them from extra confirmation steps. ([$3M funnel 15:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=935s), [Raw $5m/mo 45:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2754s), [$116K/day landing page 16:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=989s))
5. **Put a reschedule link in every message.** Without one, people cancel and never rebook. ([$3M funnel 17:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1027s))
6. **Engagement predicts a qualified show** (data from 1,000+ closes). Opened no email and replied to no text: about 3%. Engaged on only one channel: about 14–16%. Opened the email and replied to the rep's text: 42%. The "8 questions" video frames the same numbers slightly differently (no open 16%, no reply 14%, both 42%). No reply to the rep's text within 30 minutes lowers priority. ([100,000 sales calls 3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=216s), [$116K/day landing page 15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=959s), [$3M funnel 15:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=905s))
7. **Use the engagement "heat score" to protect top reps.**
    - Lead reps take only calls where the lead both opened and replied.
    - Cold leads move to newer reps or a setter to pre-qualify and confirm.
    - The roughly 20% who do neither are deleted after a few hours, as likely fake, freeing about 20% of rep time and handing the slot to a responsive prospect.
    
    ([8 questions 13:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=836s), [$116K/day landing page 16:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=989s), [100,000 sales calls 4:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=278s))
8. **Double-book to cover no-shows.** Top reps get double- or triple-booked hours (pick the better prospect using pre-call information), dedicated setters and pre-confirmed calls. One top rep can carry about 475 confirmed-looking calls a month. Swap a weaker rep's confirmed call into the top rep's unconfirmed slot. Run a "hot transfer" channel so no booked call is ever dropped; one dropped call produced a bad Trustpilot review. ([$5M/mo sales system 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=92s), [Sales onboarding 7:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=461s), [9-figure sales teams 31:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1889s))
9. **Pre-call content builds trust before the call.** Use a prep video (on the thank-you page and as a YouTube link), a 30-slide case-study deck segmented by prospect type, founder videos and the pitch deck. Sophisticated buyers check your social profiles after booking, and organic content does that credibility work. ([$3M funnel 13:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=814s), [Case studies 20:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1244s), [5 mistakes $30k/mo 24:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=1459s))
10. **Diagnose show, no-show, reschedule and cancel separately.** Have reps fill in a two-second post-call form, then build a heat map of show-up by time of day. One two-hour block ran at 10% against 80% elsewhere, so team training moved into that block. Booking 45-minute calls instead of 60-minute ones adds about three calls per rep per day, shortens time to call and lifts show-up; about 90% of sales closed within 45 minutes. ([9-figure sales teams 36:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2194s), [9-figure sales teams 25:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1548s), [9-figure sales teams 26:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1579s))
11. **Optimising Meta for "booked call" skews toward people with free time.** High-value prospects guard their calendars. Weight show and SQL data over booking counts. ([$1m/mo adspend 3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=185s), [$1M FB ROAS 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=0s))

> "The leads suck argument is absolutely not true." ([Every number tracked 2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=154s))

**For G&L:**
- Show slots 1–4 days out. Don't offer same-hour slots for cold Meta leads (no time to warm them), and don't let sellers book 2+ weeks out. Test whether seller calls do better with a slightly longer window that leaves room to gather financials.
- Reminder stack: instant confirmation email with a prep video and "what to have ready" → advisor iMessages 4 minutes later → email 24h before → SMS 4h before → email 1h before → SMS when the meeting starts. Every message has a Zoom link and a reschedule link. For 55+ sellers, keep the tone formal and the touches light: no 5–8 emails a day.
- Score heat: anyone who opened the email and replied to the advisor goes to the senior advisor. Silent bookings go to a coordinator who confirms by phone, and are released if unconfirmed a few hours before the slot.
- Pre-call pack. For buyers: a 2-minute "how seller finance works" video, 3 deal tombstones, and an FAQ on personal guarantees and bank/SBA vs vendor notes. For sellers: a "what buyers look for" one-pager, a sample valuation report (redacted), and testimonials from owners aged 55–70.
- Make sure Google and review results for "G&L M&A Advisory" show proof, because cautious sellers research you after booking.
- Track show rate per advisor and per time slot. Coach low-show advisors on the top advisor's pre-call messages. Book 45-minute calls, and double-book top advisors' hours once show rates are known.

---

<a id="d-sales"></a>

## D. Sales: The Call, Objections, Hiring Reps and Running the Team

### D1. The sales call and script (structure, stages, discovery, pitch, close)

**Why calls matter, and who should be on them**
1. Sales decides whether the funnel works. Clients running the same ads, tracking and creative get very different cost per result because of how the offer is presented on the call, so the CEO must stay directly involved. Ad metrics mean nothing until the call closes: a $500 cost per call is only good if sales are behind it. ([Business With My Wife Q&A 8:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=526s); [$1M on FB ads 11:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=677s))
2. Anything over about $2k needs a sales call, because the call customises the marketing. The more customised the offer is (or seems), the more you can charge. Every buyer goes through a call, organic buyers included, because both sides need to check fit. ([Roasted 6-Figure Owner 8:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=498s); [Explaining the $1m/mo offer 14:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=897s))
3. The founder sells first and keeps selling longer than feels comfortable. Daniel took calls himself for about two months before hiring, because without that he couldn't set KPIs, expected closes, process, prospect profile or rep pay. In month one he closed every call himself ($120k on about $50k of ads). **His view of when to stop moved over time:** in one video he left at month 4–5 and says he should have stayed a full year; in another he stopped at $250k/month and now says founders should stay until at least $500k/month; elsewhere he calls handing sales to a $20k/month manager without product experience, about six months in, a top-5 mistake. ([$100-Person Team 3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=186s); [Every revenue level 8:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=526s); [Business With My Wife Q&A 2:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=126s); [Top 5 mistakes 4:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=274s))
4. While the founder is selling, log every objection and change the offer so that objection stops coming up, instead of just moving on to the next call. ([$100K vs $1M offers 12:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=762s))

**Before the call (the system does half the selling)**
5. The prospect should arrive having seen content and ads, completed the application, been warmed and qualified by a setter, received several reminders and seen proof repeatedly. The closer's job is to finish what the system started: moving an interested but undecided prospect off the fence. "Put the best rep in the world in a broken system and he'll still lose deals." ([Close $1M/mo as a rep 1:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=94s); [100,000 sales calls 16:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=984s), [2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=123s))
6. Book calls 2–7 days out, never weeks; most people don't plan more than 4–5 workdays ahead. Within 5 minutes of a booking, send a confirmation email and an intro text from the assigned rep that asks a qualifying question ("send me your social links"), plus the prep video as a YouTube link (about 25% more views than the page alone). Ideally a setter calls to confirm, gather notes and offer an earlier slot. Don't text a prospect whose call is 30 minutes away, or one who booked at 2am. ([Landing page $116K/day 13:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=804s), [14:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=865s); [Funnel made me $3M 14:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=875s); [Raw conversation $5m/mo 44:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2662s))
7. Pre-call engagement predicts quality (data from 1,000+ closes). A lead who opens no email and answers no text has about a 3% chance of showing up qualified; one channel only, 14–16%; opened the email and replied to the rep's text, 42%. The top rep's 84% show rate (team 48%) came from talking to prospects before the call. Reply to engaged leads with thanks and a screenshot of their website to show you've done your homework. ([Landing page $116K/day 15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=959s); [$5M/mo sales system 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=153s); [100,000 sales calls 4:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=278s))
8. Build a case-study deck (about 30 slides) segmented by prospect type. Use it as pre-call material and pull it up live, so reps always have relevant proof. ([Case studies make me millions 20:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1244s))
9. Book 45-minute calls, not 60. In two years of 7th Level data about 90% of sales closed within 45 minutes and only 8% after. Shorter slots add about three calls per rep per day and lift show-up. ([9-figure sales teams 26:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1579s))

**Opening**
10. B2B opening: don't use the soft "I don't even know if we can help you." Be open that you're a salesperson there to get things done. Tested opener: "I've done my research, looked at your website and socials, been looking forward to this, I know we can absolutely help you, you came to the right place." Set the agenda, including that a decision will be made at the end. If they resist, name it ("this is a sales call") and either take control or let them leave and save the hour. ([Close $1M/mo as a rep 2:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=157s); [100,000 sales calls 18:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1109s))
11. B2C opening: warm, curious, trusted-advisor framing. Set the decision expectation gently ("learn where you are, share what we do, if it fits talk next steps"), and switch modes if a business owner turns personal and vulnerable. Match energy, pace and directness within the first seconds. ([100,000 sales calls 20:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1232s), [16:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=984s))
12. Confirm each application answer at the start ("You said $1–5k budget? 3–5 years in business? Starting within 30 days?"). The prospect committed in writing, which pre-empts the timing objection at the close. Show that you know more than they do (from research and thousands of similar calls) so they let you lead. ([8 questions 2,629% 13:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=806s); [Close $1M/mo as a rep 4:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=281s))
    - *Contradiction:* in a conversation with Matt (7th Level), Daniel endorses hiding application answers from closers, because rep bias about who will close becomes self-fulfilling (his reps avoided real-estate leads that had closed five times that month). Reconcile this by confirming the answers on the call without letting them change how much effort the rep puts in. ([9-figure sales teams 33:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2012s))

**Discovery (where calls are won or lost)**
13. Build discovery around BANT (budget, authority, need, timing) so all four classic objections are handled before the close. Every question is advance objection handling that collects "ammunition" to quote back at the close. High pressure doesn't work on wealthy buyers; the decision should feel like their own idea. ([$3M sales script 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=30s); [100,000 sales calls 23:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1416s))
14. Leverage shifts during the call. The rep has the most leverage in discovery, before any program information, price or start date; the prospect has it after the pitch. If a prospect won't be open in discovery, disqualify them: "If you're not willing to have an open and honest conversation, I don't know how my team can help you." ([Timing objections 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=61s), [1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=93s))
15. The six-question decision flowchart (it took a year and thousands of calls to build; new reps learn every branch in a few hours). Code answers as goal-based (weaker) or problem-based (stronger), and mark the disqualification points. ([$3M sales script 2:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=152s), [29:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1798s))
    - Opener: "Give me an idea of what prompted you to reach out?" Keep it broad, because narrow questions limit the answers. ([100,000 sales calls 20:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1201s))
    - **Q1 Authority:** "Is it just you, or do you have a team?" Double down on stock answers ("no one else is helping you? An assistant, a COO, a spouse on weekends?"). If someone else should be there, reschedule with them on the call. If not, make the prospect say why not. ([$3M sales script 1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=60s), [4:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=243s))
    - **Need, quantified:** "What's not working?" Put a number on the direct cost and the opportunity cost, and ask how long it has been going on. The ideal answer is "it's expensive and I've had enough." If they don't know the cost, benchmark against someone winning in their space. ([$3M sales script 5:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=303s); [Timing objections 8:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=524s))
    - **Q2 Strategy (his favourite):** "What's been your strategy so far?" Both answers help: no strategy (you provide one) or a strategy that isn't working. Follow with "how's it going?" and "what makes you look for help now?" ([$3M sales script 8:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=519s); [100,000 sales calls 36:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2188s))
    - **Q3 Dream outcome:** "Is there someone in your space whose results you wish you had?" Dig into the details like describing a dream house, and make the gap as big and concrete as possible so the price looks like an international flight, not a walk. If they have no reference point: "What makes you think this will work for you?" If they still can't answer, give a stretch timeline as a soft disqualifier. ([$3M sales script 10:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=611s), [11:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=703s); [100,000 sales calls 38:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2312s))
    - **Q4 Finances, indirectly:** never ask about revenue or bank balance. Ask "How do you get leads now?", which sits one step from ability to pay, then "Why not just double that?" to bring out the real limits. Stretch small ambitions ("what do phase 2, 3, 10 look like?"); if they truly want nothing more, disqualify. ([$3M sales script 14:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=855s), [17:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1038s))
    - **Q5 Performance numbers:** ask for the exact figure (ROAS 4x? 8x? 20x?), which marks you as an expert. Blaming "market conditions" helps you, because it shows they lack control. Future-pace: "What's the plan if nothing changes in 6 months?" If they name a plan B, push away ("honestly that sounds easier, go do that") until they admit why it won't work. ([$3M sales script 19:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1160s), [21:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1282s))
    - **Q6 Timing:** "When are you looking to implement?" Ask it as one of the last questions before the pitch (15–30 minutes in); asked early it sounds like a tie-down. Both "later" and a reflexive "right now" are wrong answers (see D2). ([Timing objections 2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=154s); [$3M sales script 22:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1343s))
16. Make them say "why now" out loud. Good answers: a new hire freed up time, a newly discovered constraint, seeing testimonials. Once they've said their reason, they won't contradict themselves 15 minutes later. If they're new to this, state the work honestly ("about 5 hours a month of your time at the start; realistic?"). If no, disqualify; if yes, "Why? Prove it to me." If they've tried before, ask what failed, what it cost and for how long, so your pitch doesn't sound like the one that failed. ([Timing objections 10:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=645s), [9:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=584s); [$3M sales script 24:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1494s))
17. Push beats pull. "People run out of a burning house faster than they run towards their dream home." Business owners' pain is always money, so skip emotional pain-digging and go straight to what acting or not acting is worth. ([Brutally honest advice 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VhlBAF8isF8&t=30s); [Close $1M/mo as a rep 8:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=498s))
18. B2B discovery should feel like two business people talking: 5–10 minutes, mostly confirming what you already know. Owners spot rigid question sequences, mirroring, looping back and consumer-style tie-downs, and trust drops once they see the process, so answer their questions directly. B2C discovery takes 30–45 minutes; rushing it is "like proposing on the first date." You won't get through every branch in an hour, so the skill is choosing which to dig into, which to skim and which to skip. ([Close $1M/mo as a rep 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=30s), [6:43](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=403s), [7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=466s); [100,000 sales calls 25:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1509s), [28:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=1724s))
19. With cold traffic, sell what the prospect already believes and does; you can't win a "world-bending" belief change. Anchor on their own success ("What % of calls do you close?" "90% of referrals." "Then we just need to get you on the phone with more people."). Diagnostic selling, such as spotting underpricing as the real bottleneck, shows you understand their economics. ([Selling cold traffic 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fs_1ndiqpHU&t=30s), [2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fs_1ndiqpHU&t=154s); [Math behind profit margins 6:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W0pUXPxvQyI&t=370s))
20. Teaching ability caps revenue. The prospect not understanding your service is expected, and that gap is why they'd hire you. If you can't explain your principles to a 10-year-old, you can't sell upmarket. Blaming an "uninformed" prospect for a lost sale is a victim mindset. ([Being "smart" 1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tCNqyltvwPI&t=62s), [4:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tCNqyltvwPI&t=277s), [0:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tCNqyltvwPI&t=32s))

**Pitch**
21. B2B: weave the pitch through the whole call. Drop a case study the moment a pain appears and a solution the moment a problem is named, so by the time you give the price they have 5–10 reasons. Don't save proof for a big reveal; be ready to give the price early. If a prospect is sceptical, giving the price (sometimes the pitch) in the first 5 minutes builds trust. B2C: pitch second to last, mapping every benefit to a problem they stated in their own words ("oven, not microwave"). ([Close $1M/mo as a rep 9:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=590s), [10:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=652s); [100,000 sales calls 57:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3424s), [59:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3580s))
22. ROI justification is the key to high-ticket B2B: if the buyer believes "it's $10–50k but I'll make $200–900k," the deal is done. Show who's "on the other side of the fence": sales picks the account manager by industry and personality fit, and meeting them closes prospects even if they don't love the rep. ([Scaling to $10M/Month 58:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3524s); [Every revenue level 42:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=2540s))
23. One offer at one price, so every call reaches the same conclusion. Removing a $6k downsell (about 20% of revenue but about 80% of customer count) took them from $1.4M to $3M the next month, with the same number of purchases at the higher price. Reps had been fumbling by "saving the sale" with the cheap option, and a second price makes prospects "go home and think." Three offers with five sub-tiers led prospects to ask the rep "what do you think we should do?" Customise after the sale, in delivery. The only exception is in-house financing, which is pitched the same way. ([Harsh business lessons 3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=183s); [Iman Gadzhi call 6:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=404s); [Raw conversation $5m/mo 38:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2325s); [Top 5 mistakes 5:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=335s), [6:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=397s))
24. A same-day bonus works: they split an already-included deliverable out as "if you're ready to move forward / bring your team to the call, we can do this special thing." That roughly doubled revenue per lead, though seasoned reps needed time to adapt. ([Made $3M in one month 14:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uci8AF7uU9A&t=866s))
25. Selling is a game of expectations ("expectations equals success"). Promise outcomes that are realistic and quick to reach, and never promise what fulfilment can't deliver. Any style is fine within that. If you don't believe your offer is the buyer's best option, go sell for someone else; prospects hear conviction. ([Explaining the $1m/mo offer 15:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=958s); [Sales hiring playbook 17:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=1022s); [$5M/mo objection overcomes 9:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=584s))

**Close and payment**
26. Close with authority. Never hand control back with "what do you think we should do next?" If the rep is unsure of the next step, the prospect will be too. B2B: state the price plainly without softening and treat it as a challenge ("you've wanted the price all call, here it is"). B2C: "Do you think what we discussed is the solution to get you XYZ?", then give the price in the same conversational tone. Use no false urgency, because owners see through it. ([Close $1M/mo as a rep 13:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=839s), [15:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=933s); [100,000 sales calls 1:04:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3860s))
27. Make the risk-free trial the default for undecided prospects. A 7-day money-back trial on calls raised top-line revenue 30%, and the "no objection, I'll start next week" stall had been costing 30–40% of deals. Not deciding should feel like turning down a free trial. ([Emails made me $720K 3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SGeD3rpjlBA&t=183s); [Every revenue level 36:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=2172s); [100,000 sales calls 1:27:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5246s))
28. Payment ladder, offering one rung at a time, top first: (1) paid in full by wire at 15% off; (2) half now, half in 1–4 weeks at 5% off (a decoy that makes paying in full look better); (3) first month as a deposit, then weekly payments from day 28, with a setup fee and in-house financing; (4) third-party financing over a year or more. Set financed prices so net cash after fees is equal. ([100,000 sales calls 1:08:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4109s); [Scaling to $10M/Month 1:12:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4348s))
29. Collect payment and the contract on the call; more deals are lost to "I'll pay right after" than are won in follow-up. Put the contract and payment link on one page. Before the call ends (5–10 minutes), the client should have signed, joined Slack and booked the account manager onboarding call. The rep then records a voice memo of about a minute for the handoff. ([100,000 sales calls 1:12:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4356s); [64 tools 11:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=711s); [Funnel made me $3M 23:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1397s), [23:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1427s))
30. Record every call (Fathom) for compliance and to capture what the client was promised. Every VSL line should come from something said on a sales call that made money. ([Case studies make me millions 13:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=808s); [64 tools 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=30s); [$10M VSL 3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=218s))
31. Follow-up: some reps close up to half their deals here. For about a month, the rep sends personalised calls, texts and emails tied to the specific objection ("how did the board meeting go?", "we've expanded financing"). Any sign of interest gets an immediate phone call, never an email thread. After a month, SDRs call every paid-for lead that didn't convert. ([100,000 sales calls 1:28:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5308s), [1:30:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5400s))

> "Stop pretending this isn't a sales call because it is." ([16:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=995s))
> "You don't have a business problem... You have a traffic problem." ([16:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=977s))
> "If you are a sales representative, you have to tell them what they should do." ([6:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=397s))

**For G&L:**
- Build a G&L six-question flowchart for **acquirers**: (1) Authority: "Are you buying alone, or with a partner, spouse or investor?" (2) Strategy: "What have you done so far to find or fund a deal (brokers, banks, cold outreach)? How's that going?" (3) Dream outcome: "Who do you know who has bought a business you wish you owned? What does it throw off?" (4) Finances, indirectly: "How are you funding your life now? What would you put toward a deal and its costs?" Never ask for a bank balance. (5) Numbers: "What size of EBITDA and which sectors are you targeting? Why not just go to a bank?" (6) Timing: "When do you want your first deal under LOI, and why now?"
- Build a parallel flowchart for **55+ sellers**: "Who else has a say: spouse, co-owner, children in the business, accountant?"; "What's your plan if you're still running this in 3 years?" (push); "Who in your circle exited well, and what did they get?" (pull); "What do you think it's worth, and what would you need to walk away?"; "Why look at this now (health, burnout, a buyer approached you)?"
- Open with homework: before the call, the rep pulls the seller's website, Companies House/ABN filings and reviews, or the buyer's LinkedIn, then opens with "I've looked at your business; you're in the right place." Confirm the application answers (capital available, timeline, EBITDA band) in the first 2 minutes.
- For sellers, put the cost of waiting in numbers: lost multiple as owner-dependence grows, and the years of profit at risk if health or the market turns. For buyers: the salary or ownership income forgone every month without a deal.
- Weave proof as soon as a pain appears: keep a case-study deck segmented into "first-time buyer, under 10% down", "vendor-financed trade business" and "owner who exited at 60". Show the deal team (the analyst or advisor who will run their file) before the price.
- Sell one core engagement at one price, with a payment ladder (PIF discount → split → deposit plus monthly) rather than a cheaper "lite" tier. Collect the deposit and e-signature on the call and book the onboarding session before hanging up.
- Use 45-minute calls, booked within 2–7 days, with a rep text within 5 minutes; route non-responders to a setter for reconfirmation.

### D2. Objection handling (timing, money, spouse, think-about-it, etc.)

**Principles**
1. Every objection at the close reveals a mistake made earlier in discovery, and every objection reduces to budget, authority, need or timing. Handle them in discovery while you still have leverage. "You're not here to overcome the objection. You're here to understand why," and you have no business pitching until you do. ([100,000 sales calls 1:15:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4540s); [Timing objections 7:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=431s))
2. Tag every lost call as B, A, N or T, and don't hang up without a reason. Find the category that loses the most deals, find the rep who rarely gets it, and copy them. Then fix it structurally: for authority, every call now opens with "Who else from your team needs to be on this call?", which raised closes team-wide. Budget losses led to three new financing options and a B2B buy-now-pay-later provider. Frequent timing losses call for better targeting or a free month for signing now. ([$5M/mo objection overcomes 8:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=522s); [Sales hiring playbook 8:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=494s); [Roasted 6-Figure Owner 1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=93s), [2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=154s); [64 tools 14:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=895s))
3. Turn objections into offer, VSL and ad changes. "Every part of your offer must overcome the objections you hear." Rebuild the VSL's "how it works" section as a series of objection answers. Grow the offer one objection at a time (each addition opened a colder audience and justified a higher price). Turn objections into ad angles ("ads don't work for me" became "get clients without paid ads"). When a prospect says exactly what must happen before they'd buy, treat that as offer research. ([$100K vs $1M offers 12:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=762s), [14:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=884s), [17:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=1037s), [18:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=1099s); [$10M VSL 7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3-WQstsN5I&t=433s))
4. The most dangerous objections are the ones you never hear ("this looks like a scam"), because those people never book or show up. Fix them in the website, testimonials, reminders and a brand-neutral review site that outranks Reddit, since cancellations are usually a reputation problem. Answer bad reviews publicly with a senior person. ([$5M/mo objection overcomes 10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=646s); [9-figure sales teams 36:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2194s); [Funnel made me $3M 8:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=498s))
5. Peel back the prospect's "locus of control" one layer at a time: concepts (economy) → things (money) → people (partner) → themselves. "I need to check with my partner" is progress compared with "it's the economy." Many "think about it" or "I'll compare" objections mean your positioning is too broad. Cold leads' B/A/T objections mostly mean "I wasn't looking for this, so I don't need it badly enough." ([$36K training 47:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=2854s); [Best version overnight 1:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VLNCU_ZM0Gk&t=112s); [$100K vs $1M offers 17:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=1037s))

**Timing ("not now", "next quarter", "immediately")**
6. Never accept a reflexive "now/immediately" at face value: "Why now? Give me three reasons. What blocked you last week, last month, last year?" If the answer is "not immediately": "What's got you blocked until then? Why are you on the call now if you're not ready to act?" ([Timing objections 2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=154s); [100,000 sales calls 49:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2990s))
7. For a legitimate blocker (holiday, wedding, staffing change, next-quarter budget, waiting on a new CMO), ask: "If we can handle that for you, would you be open to starting sooner?" Extend the programme by those weeks and start prep now, cover the missing role in the meantime, or rebook for their return. Give reps these extension tools through admin and fulfilment.
