([Timing objections 5:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=339s); [$3M sales script 26:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1585s); [100,000 sales calls 1:23:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5031s))
8. If there's no logistical blocker, just no urgency, put a number on the daily cost of delay (money wasted, appreciation missed). The guarantee clock starts when they're ready, with a deposit to hold the spot. ([100,000 sales calls 1:23:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5031s); [Guarantees 22:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OsktcdNDATU&t=1354s))
9. Window shoppers: "What specifically are you looking for? If you found exactly that, would you start today?" If they have no criteria: "If you don't know what you're looking for, how will you know when you've found it?", then disqualify. Specific criteria (e.g. "5 case studies from US doctors") are easy wins. If they won't commit, end the call and book for 3 months out, saving 30–45 minutes. Screen for this upstream: applicants starting 30+ days out (about 8%) go to free training. ([Timing objections 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=215s); [$3M sales script 27:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1646s); [8 questions 2,629% 12:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=743s))
10. Expect seasonality: around holidays, authority objections (stakeholders away) and "I'll pick this up in Q1" rise, and revenue per call drops. Plan capacity for it. ([I failed 9:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=590s))

**Budget / money**
11. A budget objection is usually doubt about value, not lack of money ("sell them a mansion for $1k and they'd find the money"). Isolate it: "Money aside, do you think this would work for you? Why?" If it's genuinely cash, use the payment ladder ("how much can you put down today?") and change the terms, not the price. If it's value doubt, use the trial: "You can't know the value from the outside; I'm so confident our team would work for free for 7 days." ([100,000 sales calls 1:16:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4601s); [$100K vs $1M offers 13:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=793s))
12. "It depends on cost" while window shopping is a smokescreen. The cheapest investment rarely gives the best return, and value is judged through testimonials, reviews and guarantees: "What's the most valuable thing you hope to get?" Any answer leads to "start with the 7-day money-back guarantee"; no answer is a hard disqualification. ([$3M sales script 29:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1767s); [100,000 sales calls 56:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=3361s))
13. Handle budget early and indirectly through lead source: referral-reliant ("you have a traffic problem, not a business problem"), paid ads ("why not double the spend?"), plan B ("cold email? Then go do that"). Answers you want: it's inconsistent, too slow, plateaued, all eggs in one basket. Sell reallocation of existing spend, not new money. ([100,000 sales calls 42:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=2559s); [$3M sales script 15:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=946s); [5 business lessons 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=215s))
14. Don't over-qualify on budget before showing value. 80% of 10-minute setter calls ended on a budget objection, against 20% of 60-minute closer calls. Asking "can you afford our minimum?" burned leads. Check that objection frequencies on setter calls match those on closer calls. (One rep does successfully pre-qualify on price, "entry price is X, is it even worth the discussion?", so judge the approach by revenue per lead.) ([100,000 sales calls 13:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=800s); [$5M/mo sales system 7:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=464s))

**Authority ("I need to talk to my partner/spouse")**
15. Prevent it in discovery (Q1 above, plus "who else needs to be on this call?"; reschedule if they should be). At the close it's usually fear and wanting someone to blame: "If your partner were fully on board, is this the right decision for you? Why?" or "If they said no but you needed it, what would you do?" Otherwise take a refundable deposit now, give risk-free access to the materials to show them, and invite the partner to the onboarding or strategy call so the work persuades them. ([$3M sales script 1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=60s); [100,000 sales calls 1:20:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4847s); [$5M/mo objection overcomes 1:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=90s))

**Need / "I'll think about it" / "I need more info"**
16. "Sounds good, let me think about it" has no objection to handle, and colder audiences avoid action, so don't try to force them. Offer your absence: "Assuming this doesn't work out, what's the plan to get this done by yourself?" Having no plan is the reason to buy. Or redefine "staying at rest": "Perfect, that's why we offer a week. Think about it inside the programme with the real team; next week I won't have anything new and you won't have better information." Declining a risk-free, this-call-only option becomes the active negative choice. ([Selling cold traffic 3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fs_1ndiqpHU&t=216s), [4:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fs_1ndiqpHU&t=279s), [5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fs_1ndiqpHU&t=340s); [$5M/mo objection overcomes 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=0s); [5 business lessons 5:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=338s))
17. "I need more information": agree, then flip it. The only way to get more is the next step (a deposit) and talking to fulfilment, not another hour with a rep. "I need to find out more before I buy" becomes "If I want to find out more, I need to buy." ([$5M/mo objection overcomes 1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=60s); [$100K vs $1M offers 16:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29qOh8ZnF0k&t=976s))
18. "Not sure I need it" means the gap is too small. You can't move the destination after the pitch, but you can move the starting point: "Maybe stay where you are for a couple more years?" Then use the guarantee. "I don't have time" early on is a red flag: "If we did most of the heavy lifting, would that work?" Anyone who insists on zero involvement isn't a buyer. ([100,000 sales calls 1:21:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4909s); [$3M sales script 6:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=365s))
19. Stalling owners / "let's follow up later": "I'm sure you don't take these calls to window shop; your time is valuable and so is mine." Reps show their fully double-booked week ("every other owner on my calendar shows up ready to decide") to show they aren't desperate, while using no false urgency. ([100,000 sales calls 1:06:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=4016s); [Close $1M/mo as a rep 14:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=870s))

**Proof and trust objections**
20. "Prove why I should do this": anchor on the hour they've already invested ("what about our ads, landing page or your business made you believe it was worth it?"). "Prove you're an expert in my industry": don't try. "You're the expert in your industry; we're the experts in [our field]." ([Selling cold traffic 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fs_1ndiqpHU&t=92s); [$5M/mo objection overcomes 1:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=90s))
21. "Let me call one of your clients": never hand out client contacts. Of 15 prospects who said a client call would make them "100% in," only 1 signed; it's an exit excuse. "I can't give you my clients' contact info, but I promise I won't give yours out either." ([$5M/mo objection overcomes 2:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=121s))
22. "Get me results first, then I'll pay": defend hard or end the call. Point to 1,000+ clients and reviews, then reframe the real question from "does this work?" to "will it work for me?", which is about their self-belief, and end optimistically. ([$5M/mo objection overcomes 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=215s))
23. "I'm not in your industry / show me a relevant case": they want the outcome, not the same industry. Pick the case-study angle that matches their pain. "I'll compare with others": "Who else are you talking to who does the same thing?" In a narrow niche the answer is no one, and you can show competitors on the call when the comparison clearly favours you. ([Case studies make me millions 17:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=1059s); [Best version overnight 1:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VLNCU_ZM0Gk&t=112s), [3:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VLNCU_ZM0Gk&t=236s))

**The guarantee and trial as a universal handler (and its failure mode)**
24. The 7-day refundable deposit/trial answers almost everything. Too expensive: "just a deposit, see the ROI in a week." Partner: "bring them to the first meeting." Not sure it fits: "that's what week one is for." Timing: "the clock starts when you're ready; we'll hold your spot." ([Guarantees 22:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OsktcdNDATU&t=1354s))
25. **Evolution:** reps then used the trial as a crutch for every objection, so clients arrived unsold, and many took the week-one roadmap and left to do it themselves. A $5M month lost almost $2M to refunds when fulfilment couldn't deliver inside the window. Fixes: refunds only by telling the account manager on a recorded onboarding call (Planet Fitness logic), no commission on refunds, fines for too many back-outs, and the trial offered conditionally to some prospects only. It only works when sales is honest and week one is strong. ([Every revenue level 38:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=2295s); [$100-Person Team 25:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=1545s); [Guarantees 24:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OsktcdNDATU&t=1480s); [Funnel made me $3M 10:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=624s))

> "If everything you offered was not enough, offer your absence." ([0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qv1VrleLB08&t=30s))
> "Hesitation, left alone, almost always unfortunately defaults to a lost sale." ([21:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OsktcdNDATU&t=1263s))
> "We never accept right now as an answer… despite those being positive answers." ([2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nfz4L5zQrDo&t=124s))

**For G&L:**
- **"I don't have the capital"** (buyer): isolate it. "Money aside, if we found a business that paid for itself through vendor finance, is this right for you? Why?" Then reframe: "You don't have a capital problem, you have a deal-structure problem. That's exactly what we solve." Use the payment ladder for G&L's own fee (PIF discount → split → deposit plus monthly). Never cut the price.
- **"I need to talk to my accountant/lawyer"** (seller or buyer): prevent it in Q1 ("Who advises you on this: accountant, lawyer, spouse? Should they be on this call?") and reschedule with them. At the close: "If your accountant agreed, is this the right move for you? Why?" Then take a refundable deposit and invite the accountant to the onboarding/valuation session, where the work persuades them.
- **"Not ready to sell yet / maybe in 2–3 years"** (55+ owner): treat it as the blocker branch. "If we use the next 12 months to make you exit-ready, so the timeline is yours, would you start the prep now?" Put the cost of waiting in numbers: multiple lost to owner-dependence, health, and market risk.
- **"Let me think about it"**: "Assuming you don't work with us, what's your plan to find, structure and finance a deal yourself?" Then offer a short refundable onboarding window (if compliant for advisory services) so they "think about it" with the deal team.
- **"Can I speak to one of your past clients?"**: decline on confidentiality grounds (M&A clients especially value it): "I won't share their details, and I'll never share yours." Offer anonymised deal case studies instead.
- **"Is no-money-down even legit / sounds like a scam"**: you'll mostly never hear this one. Answer it in the VSL, the pre-call deck and a review hub, with real closed-deal structures.
- Tag every lost G&L call B/A/N/T in the CRM, review it monthly, and turn the top loss reason into a new VSL section, ad angle or offer component (e.g. a lender/vendor-finance introduction for budget losses).

### D3. Hiring sales reps (profile, sourcing, screening, comp, onboarding)

**When and whom to hire**
1. Sequence: founder sells → first hire is an SDR/assistant to manage the calendar, disqualify non-fits and handle pre-call contact → full-time closers once you can fill their calendars → then add closers and SDRs alternately, depending on the bottleneck (today about 20 closers and 24 SDRs). **Contradiction:** in another video he says they reached $3M/month with closers only and doesn't think setters are needed before about $3M/month. At $700k/year, a founder taking discovery calls is low-value use of their time, and a setter costs about $60–80k/year. ([Built my $3M/month team 2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=123s); [$100-Person Team 4:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=249s), [5:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=312s); [Funnel made me $3M 17:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=1057s); [Roasted 6-Figure Owner 3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cZQ4hJNpW7o&t=186s))
2. Hire experienced reps who train themselves and can close hundreds of thousands to $1M/month from the start ("batteries included"). Unless you're a sales-training company, training reps isn't your job. His first dozen reps burned hundreds of thousands while he hoped they'd improve in months 2–5, and they didn't. At $500k/month he had about 10 weak reps; today any rep under $500k/month is underperforming. ([Built my $3M/month team 4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=246s); [Hire $3m/mo reps 15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=959s); [Every revenue level 11:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=714s); [Sales onboarding tutorial 11:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=684s))
3. Fewer, better reps. $500k–$1.2M/month can come from one excellent rep with the right controls; 20–30 reps at about $100k each create commission, call-review and admin mess at a tenth of the efficiency. One A-player beats five B-players, so overpay for top talent. Two good sellers should reach $300–500k/month. ([$5M/mo sales system 9:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=590s); [Made $3M in one month 10:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uci8AF7uU9A&t=619s); [AMA $3M/mo 14:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=871s))
4. Match reps' credibility to the buyer. Reps who presented like peers of the 30–40-year-old owners they sold to (polish, gravitas, image) produced about 5× the revenue per day of less-seasoned reps, and reps who out-earn prospects carry authority. The reverse also holds: a rep with hundreds of thousands of followers failed because prospects felt "you're too far ahead," and she now thrives making ad creative. *(Compliance note: hire on presentation, relevant experience and credibility, never on age or other protected traits; Daniel's own screen says "ignore age.")* ([Raw conversation $5m/mo 41:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2478s); [Built my $3M/month team 8:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=524s); [Business lessons 3M/mo 1:12:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=4347s))
5. Elite reps need flexibility, not rigid structure ("knowing how to sell" vs "running a sales process"). Hire self-motivated "hunters" who regulate themselves. Top 5 reps never asked a question beyond the onboarding material. ([Close $1M/mo as a rep 12:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=774s); [Raw conversation $5m/mo 49:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2998s); [Sales onboarding tutorial 5:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=309s))
6. Hire closers from your biggest competitor, then ask exactly what was pitched in that competitor's best-closing offer and build that talk track into yours. ([Won't make 1M/mo 2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=154s))
7. Setting needs real skill, and more of it the higher the ticket; done badly it burns the lead before the closer speaks. One peer renamed "setter" as a client-support or admin-coordinator role and hired professional-services admin staff and law students as KPI'd W2 employees, who outperformed young setters selling to millionaires. ([100,000 sales calls 14:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=891s); [Scaling to $10M/Month 55:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3307s))

**Sourcing (recruit like you sell)**
8. Referrals first: 7 of 12 reps came from them ("good reps know good reps"). Optionally pay a $5,000 bonus if the hire stays 90 days. Second, social: a text-on-screen story asking for a video intro brings 100+ applications a month, and YouTube about rep earnings attracts talent. From scratch, use every job board and budget several thousand dollars; expect hundreds of applicants for 1–2 solid reps. A staffing or placement agency ($5–10k fee) can recruit, train and place reps experienced in your niche; hire two and keep the better one. ([Hire $3m/mo reps 0:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=32s), [1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=92s), [3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=185s); [AMA $3M/mo 13:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=839s))
9. Treat recruiting like a sales funnel with a big claim plus proof. The careers page shows the last 90 days of every full-time rep's leads, deals, revenue and commission (average about $20k/month, top reps about $40k). Disclosing real earnings is the biggest single lever; publicising one rep's $70k month nearly emptied a competitor's team. Job-post language that works: 100% inbound B2B leads, full pipeline, short cycle, performance culture, mentorship, top call recordings, a one-week ramp, a near-seven-figure top end. Also sell the company's stability and growth story. ([Sales hiring playbook 2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=124s), [1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=61s), [11:49](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=709s); [$5M/mo sales system 13:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=805s); [$100-Person Team 8:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=528s))
10. Write a detailed job description so candidates self-disqualify, and pair it with a pre-recorded expectations video. If you're unknown, overpay ("the tax for being unknown," about double what he pays). ([Hire $3m/mo reps 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=215s); [Sales onboarding tutorial 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=0s); [$0 to $500K checklist 3:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AZC82IKCiVI&t=188s))

**Screening**
11. The application is a Loom intro of 5 minutes or less (a Google Drive link is an instant disqualification because it shows weak tech skills) plus 3 questions: most recent relevant experience (ideally selling online to high-level owners); "What is the most important part of a sales call? Be specific" (tests a concise, eloquent argument); and "What have you changed your mind about in selling?" (the only wrong answer is "I've never been wrong"). ([Sales hiring playbook 2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=155s); [Hire $3m/mo reps 6:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=401s))
12. Screen for professional presentation, since calls are on video and an owner must trust the rep with tens of thousands of dollars. Disqualify shaky phones, old webcams, bedrooms or messy backgrounds, and bad audio (about 1 in 5 is deleted for audio). Decisions happen in the first 30 seconds, watched at 1.5×. Ignore "entrepreneurial since a teen" and "I work harder than anyone"; look for proof they've made a company money through sales, or an honest account of how their experience lets them ramp fast. Look for professional, motivated, coachable people. Overconfident openers are a no. Auto-delete applicants who brag about a high close rate. ([Hire $3m/mo reps 7:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=465s), [10:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=618s), [11:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=679s), [12:51](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=771s); [1.8% close rate 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=30s))
13. Often there's no interview: a clear Loom means a hire on the spot, and the follow-up call is for the candidate's questions, which also shows their discovery skill. "Everyone sounds great in an interview"; live leads reveal the truth. **Contradiction on group interviews:** one video describes a staffing firm running a weekly group interview of five vetted candidates (hiring 1–2), and a peer uses group interviews → shortlist → 1:1 → cohort onboarding. In "Hire $3m/mo reps" Daniel says to avoid group calls because they look unprofessional and annoy star reps, and to keep it private over email. ([Hire $3m/mo reps 14:57](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=897s), [5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=308s); [Sales hiring playbook 4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=248s); [Sales onboarding tutorial 8:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=524s); [I failed 19:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=1150s); [Scaling to $10M/Month 59:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3554s))

**Compensation**
14. OTE = quota × commission %. Pick the OTE that attracts the calibre you want, then raise quota (better product, process and leads) faster than you lower the percentage. Set an honest starting OTE from your own numbers: last month's sales you closed × about 10% (the remote-sales standard), and expect a hire to get about half your results. ([Manage $3M/mo sales team 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-TtDt092fQg&t=61s), [5:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-TtDt092fQg&t=339s); [Hire $3m/mo reps 4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=246s))
15. A high percentage is a red flag to good reps. Strong businesses pay about 1% or less; unproven startups 15% plus equity; MLMs 20–50%. Viral Coach pays 5% (industry about 10%) and reps still earn about twice market rate (5% × realistic quota = $20–30k months). A manager who demanded 30% came back months later asking for 7%. Total sales comp, including management and SDRs, runs just over 5% of top line (about 6% in another video), about half the industry norm. ([Manage $3M/mo sales team 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-TtDt092fQg&t=0s), [2:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-TtDt092fQg&t=122s), [4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-TtDt092fQg&t=246s), [5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-TtDt092fQg&t=308s); [$100-Person Team 7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=467s))
16. Tier the commission by monthly volume, paid in the month closed (retention is fulfilment's job). One version: $250–500k closed → $1,500/deal, over $500k → $2,000/deal. Another: stair-steps at under $250k / $250–500k / $500k+, with the higher rate applied retroactively to every deal that month. Top reps earn $40–70k/month; some are on track for about $500k/year. Flat per-deal pay kills competitiveness: low performers hit $4–5k, cover rent, check out and burn the rest of the month's paid leads. ([Sales hiring playbook 14:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=898s); [$100-Person Team 7:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=436s), [9:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=558s))
17. Pay on net new cash collected in the first 30 days (they collect 85% within 30 days; about 30% is a common benchmark), with clawback: no commission on refunds or wrongly closed clients. Daniel stopped paying extra commission for paid-in-full upgrades and saw no drop. ([Scaling to $10M/Month 9:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=581s), [1:24:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=5080s), [1:26:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=5172s); [Explaining the $1m/mo offer 17:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=1020s))
18. Don't change comp mid-stream. A peer who cut percentages for middle performers after a sprint lost their best closers. Their old plan (8–10% of net new cash plus a points bonus pool, with weekend dollars weighted 1.5×) pushed the total sales department to 20–25% of net cash. Moving 1099 reps to W2 at about $3–3.5M/month cut take-home by 30–40% and changed the culture; Daniel's view is that you can still sell a business that uses contractors. Promoting one rep to sales manager lost half that team overnight. *(Check local employment law on contractor vs employee status.)* ([Scaling to $10M/Month 4:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=274s), [7:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=428s), [44:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=2693s), [52:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3122s))
19. Recruit with effective hourly rate: commission month-to-date ÷ shown calls (about $400–575/hour for top reps). It is very compelling to KPI-loving reps in interviews. ([Every number I tracked 13:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=800s); [$5M/mo sales system 11:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=714s))

**Onboarding and trial**
20. Onboarding: contract → company email → Slack → the sales lead gives a handbook of about 20 pages, training videos (3–5 days) and hundreds of recorded one-call closes. Learn the pitch, then integrate with the sales lead. Finish training quickly; drag on for a week and the offer may be withdrawn. Solve your own tech problems. New reps memorise the script first, then the process, then the funnel; a memorised script frees attention to listen. ([Sales hiring playbook 16:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=960s); [Sales onboarding tutorial 4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=248s); [9-figure sales teams 38:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2318s))
21. Gate the calendar with a mock call: fail two mock calls and you go to SDR for a month. ([Sales hiring playbook 18:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=1082s))
22. Trial on live leads with a fixed budget. 100 tier-3 leads (the cheapest and hardest, at about $1,000 revenue-per-lead benchmark); pass = at least 2 closes, judged on lead efficiency, independence and selling. A variant: half the normal lead flow for about 2 weeks (about $20k), and if there are no wins by about week 3, let them go. Publish trial records (fastest close was the first lead in the first hour; best trial 5 deals; fastest graduation 2 days, 13 leads → 3 closes; best first month $46k commission on tier-3 leads). Keep a dedicated sales budget for testing reps. ([Sales onboarding tutorial 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=30s), [5:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=339s), [9:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=555s); [Hire $3m/mo reps 15:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=929s); [Sales hiring playbook 13:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=835s))
23. Failing closers can move to SDR work (setting, or following up near-free leads) and retry later; the top SDR made $24k commission in a month. Long-term follow-up on old paid leads is a low-stakes training ground. A career ladder (note-taker → tier-1 closer → manager) helps retention. ([Sales hiring playbook 14:27](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=867s); [100,000 sales calls 1:30:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=5400s); [Scaling to $10M/Month 59:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=3554s))
24. Expect churn and over-hire for it. Of about 50 reps who started a trial in a year, 5 remain (about 10%). Always be hiring for KPI-measured roles, and always hire at least two for any new seat so you can compare. Expect the rep who sticks to be within your first ~10 hires, and expect to micromanage your first rep. ([Won't make 1M/mo 2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=154s); [Made $3M in one month 9:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uci8AF7uU9A&t=556s); [5 business lessons 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=30s); [AMA $3M/mo 14:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=871s))

> "OTE equals quota times percentage. This is the entire formula that you need to understand to be able to attract better talent." ([1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-TtDt092fQg&t=61s))
> "Unless you are a sales training company, your job should not be training sales reps." ([15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=959s))
> "If you don't have a reputation yet, you are going to have to overpay for your talent. That is the tax for being unknown." ([3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=215s))

**For G&L:**
- **Profile:** experienced high-ticket closers who have sold $10k+ advisory, financial, M&A, business-brokerage, franchise or wealth-adjacent services to business owners. Look for financial literacy (they can talk EBITDA, multiples, vendor notes), a calm, credible presence on camera, and evidence of revenue closed. Select on credibility and experience with owners, never on age.
- **Two seats, two flavours:** seller-side calls with 55+ owners reward patient, consultative, trust-building reps (closer to the B2C-style 30–45-minute discovery). Buyer-side calls with ambitious acquirers reward direct, ROI-driven B2B closers. Trial both kinds of rep on both lead types and route by revenue per lead.
- **Sourcing:** referrals from brokers, accountants and the M&A advisory community ($5k bonus at 90 days), a careers page showing real rep earnings, and poaching closers from the biggest acquisition-education competitors, then asking what their best-closing pitch was.
- **Screen:** a Loom of 5 minutes or less plus "most important part of a sales call?" plus "what have you changed your mind about in selling?" Add one G&L question: "Explain vendor finance to a 62-year-old owner in 60 seconds."
- **Comp:** set OTE from the founder's own closes × about 10% to start; tier by monthly cash collected; pay on cash collected within 30 days with clawback on refunds. Show the candidate real effective hourly rate once you have data.
- **Onboarding:** a handbook, the G&L objection flowchart, 20+ recorded founder closes, a mock call (fail twice = setter seat), then a fixed trial of about 50–100 lower-tier leads with a pass bar of at least 2 closes. Have the founder keep closing until at least $250–500k/month so the script exists first.
- **First sales hire:** a professional setter/coordinator (admin or legal-background profile) to confirm calls, clarify applications ("you said $500k–$1M EBITDA: closer to which?") and send the deal case-study deck.

### D4. Managing a sales team (KPIs, close rates, cash collected, coaching, call reviews, culture, rep tiers)

**The numbers**
1. Manage on objective numbers, not sales psychology (Daniel is a former accountant who rebuilt the team from $500k in debt). Close rate, total revenue and revenue per call are "kindergarten" metrics; track 10–20+ data points per rep. Per-rep sheet: leads allocated, calls booked, shows, call-to-show %, qualified, show-to-qualified %, deals won, qualified close %, show-to-close %, lead-to-close ratio, revenue per sale, total revenue, revenue per call, revenue per lead, commission month-to-date, estimated monthly commission, effective hourly rate, operational cost, plus team and tier averages. ([Every number I tracked 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=0s), [1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=93s); [Made $3M in one month 5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uci8AF7uU9A&t=340s))
2. **Revenue per lead is the master KPI** (leaderboards are sorted by it). It captures follow-up and getting allocated leads onto calls, because the company pays for every lead, not just every call, and close rate can be gamed or defined too many ways. Rate reps on ROI (revenue against the cost of their leads), monthly and on a rolling 90 days. Other sales KPIs: total revenue, refunds, LTV per sale. ([$5M/mo sales system 3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=186s); [Every number I tracked 3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=185s); [9-figure sales teams 30:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1828s), [35:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2133s); [5 business lessons 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=92s))
3. **Operational drag / efficiency cost** = (best rep's revenue per lead in the tier − this rep's) × this rep's leads. A gap of about $1k/lead × 177 leads ≈ $150–185k/month. Volume hides inefficiency: one "top performer" with the highest commission took nearly double the leads at the lowest revenue per lead, about $600k of drag. This makes moving reps up or down tiers objective. ([Every number I tracked 4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=248s), [5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=340s); [$5M/mo sales system 14:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=865s))
4. **Close-rate benchmarks** (always tied to price): 12–15% of live calls; 12% in a $3.5M month ("don't count on 20–30%"); a peer at 13% of shows. Full funnel by definition: 17% qualified-show-to-close, 12% show-to-close, 8% confirmed-call-to-close, 6.6% call-to-close, 1.8% lead-to-close. A 30–40% (or 60%) close rate means your price is too low. Levers in priority order: price > leads > close rate. Going from 12% to 24% close is worth the same as halving ad cost, and creative is often quicker to fix. ([Case studies make me millions 7:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=434s); [Won't make 1M/mo 3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=185s), [5:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=339s); [1.8% close rate 8:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=530s), [1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=60s), [6:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=374s); [Raw conversation $5m/mo 46:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2815s))
5. **Price ceiling = rep morale.** Raising prices until close rate fell to 6% was more profitable than 12%, but reps got about one deal a week, lost motivation, and the price came back down. Reps need wins weekly (a couple of weeks without a check and they check out). ([Raw conversation $5m/mo 48:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2906s); [1.8% close rate 11:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=683s))
6. **Other benchmarks:** at more than $1M/month cold spend, 2,671 leads/month, about 39% DQ'd, 1,633 live calls, about 48% show; the top rep closed $1M from about 200 calls; top-rep months of $1.2M, $800k, $600k, $500k, $400k; targets of $1.2–1.5M per rep per month; reps bring in about $1M/month each while earning $50–70k. New-cash AOV is about $6,200 per close, with 85% collected within 30 days. ROAS thresholds: 3× goal, 4× "printing money", 2× means relying on the back end. ([$5M/mo sales system 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=31s), [9:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=559s); [Close $1M/mo as a rep 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=0s); [Every number I tracked 11:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=677s); [Invest in your business 4:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4VoddZacLrM&t=244s); [Scaling to $10M/Month 9:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=581s))
7. **Diagnostic KPIs:** call-to-show % shows pre-call skill (84% vs 48%; passive reps get about 30% and active ones double it, and pre-call work can double a rep's commission). Show-to-qualified % shows confidence: reps on identical leads rated 33–87% (or 73% vs 91%) as qualified, and those who believe in the leads close more. Never let that subjective "qualified" call affect pay or standing. Qualified close % shows a closing problem. Lead-to-close shows how many leads marketing must supply for one deal (best rep 10:1 vs 25:1). Track SQLs (rep-confirmed qualified shows) so marketing can optimise cost per SQL. ([Every number I tracked 7:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=461s), [8:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=491s), [9:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=584s), [10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=646s); [$5M/mo sales system 4:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=278s); [Sales onboarding tutorial 1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=91s); [$1M on FB ads 2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=123s))
8. **Forecast and plan capacity:** spend per rep ÷ CPL = leads; leads × that rep's revenue per lead = forecast (that's how they planned a $3.9M month). Work backwards from the goal (clients → calls → conversations → leads → daily ad spend). Going from $3M to $5M needed about 4 more reps and 8 more account managers. Staff above target: he staffed exactly for $5.1M, lost two reps (a fifth of capacity, including a tier-2 lead who closed about $700k and earned about $30k commission) and missed. A new rep removed after a week left calendars full with nowhere for the leads to go. ([Every number I tracked 11:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=708s); [$0 to $500K checklist 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AZC82IKCiVI&t=0s); [Built my $3M/month team 11:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=681s); [Made $3M in one month Q&A 8:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=494s); [I failed 7:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=435s), [8:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=527s))

**Lead tiers and routing**
9. Use colour-coded lead tiers with their own averages, and KPI reps against their tier, not the whole team. That kills the "leads suck" excuse: same tier, same lead quality. New reps start on tier 3 and earn promotion; reset metrics monthly and move reps between tiers, even mid-month, on revenue per lead and appointments taken. ([Every number I tracked 2:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=124s), [14:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=862s); [$5M/mo sales system 4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=248s); [Sales hiring playbook 5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=308s))
10. Route by merit, not round-robin ("Steph Curry gets the ball"). Fill the best rep's calendar first, and swap a weaker rep's confirmed call into the top rep's unconfirmed slot. Sending the best leads to the best reps took revenue per lead from $500 to $1,000 overnight. Route by application answers with historical revenue per lead: custom-domain emails and higher revenue go to top closers (about 250% throughput gain), "browsing" leads go to the weakest. Move cold, non-engaging leads from top reps to juniors or setters to reconfirm, and cancel unreachable ones after a few hours (about 20% of the day freed). The best reps take the most calls, because more practice makes them better. ([9-figure sales teams 31:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1889s), [32:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1951s); [Funnel made me $3M 2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=155s); [8 questions 2,629% 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=61s), [13:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=836s); [Landing page $116K/day 16:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=989s); [$5M/mo sales system 12:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=775s))
11. Give top-tier reps extra resources: setters pre-calling their leads, double or triple booking so a no-show doesn't waste the hour (one top rep can carry about 475 calls a month), pre-confirmed calls, and a VA for admin. ([Sales hiring playbook 6:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=371s); [$5M/mo sales system 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=92s); [Sales onboarding tutorial 7:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=461s); [9-figure sales teams 30:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1828s))
12. Don't burn an expensive lead after one rep's "no." Pass it to a rep with a different style (low-pressure vs high-leadership), since different buyers respond to different reps. When a prospect declines, the sales director emails "How could we have made this better?" to get feedback on every lost deal. ([Why easy businesses win 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=30s), [1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i4mWmsC0mTA&t=92s))

**Capacity and call hygiene**
13. Run closers at 70–80% of capacity (about 80% calendar utilisation), with double-booking and a floating closer for overflow. At 100% they cut corners and lose deals, and past a point extra ad spend stopped producing revenue because closer capacity was the bottleneck. Cap back-to-back calls at five (calls 6–8 in a row closed at about 2%), e.g. three calls, a break, three calls. Let data talk reps out of 12 calls a day ("let's try nine and do them well"). ([9-figure sales teams 19:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1175s), [23:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1425s), [24:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1455s), [29:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1766s))
14. A two-second post-call form builds a show-up heat map by time of day after a month. Put team training in the dead block (e.g. 10% show-up against 80% elsewhere). Measure setters on hours online (6–7 quality hours/day), contact leads within 5 minutes, and build legal calling rules into the system rather than trusting entry-level staff. ([9-figure sales teams 25:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1548s), [13:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=833s), [8:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=495s))
15. Never drop a booked call. If you can't take it, hand it off in a "hot transfer" Slack channel (a rep who left for lunch two minutes in earned a bad Trustpilot review, and a dropped call ends a trial). Pick up others' calls when you get a no-show; transfers are often tier 1–2 leads. ([Sales onboarding tutorial 2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=154s), [3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=185s))

**Coaching and call reviews**
16. Coach with hard facts. Highlight anyone far below the team average in red and have them copy the best performer, rather than nitpicking calls. High volume but low revenue per call → model the best closer's calls. Low revenue per lead → improve pre-call messaging and disqualification judgement. Over-DQ'ing → watch peers' calls. Weak show rate → copy the top rep's pre-call messages, which are open to the team. ([$5M/mo sales system 7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=433s); [Sales hiring playbook 7:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=432s); [Every number I tracked 8:11](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=491s))
17. **Call review: Daniel is hands-off.** He "moneyballs" with 20+ KPIs, mostly without reviewing calls, lets reps sell however they like within compliance, and doesn't impose his style if someone gets the result differently. *Nuances:* while diagnosing a crisis he did nightly "game tape" review; Matt (7th Level) is strict with new reps ("do it my way until you're top"), gives one most-important fix per review rather than 20 notes, and re-checks after a dozen calls. ([Hire $3m/mo reps 16:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M0Cc-4RO2fA&t=991s); [9-figure sales teams 35:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2133s), [40:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=2410s); [Business lessons 3M/mo 57:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3428s); [Went broke 19:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1154s))
18. The sales lead is a player-coach with integrity and management experience, not a trainer or "nanny." They run a weekly huddle (mostly admin and marketing changes), onboard new hires, act as sounding board and spokesperson, and hold standards. Promote the most experienced closer once you pass about 10 reps. The owner runs sales changes past the sales lead first. ([Built my $3M/month team 3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=216s); [$100-Person Team 6:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=375s); [Raw conversation $5m/mo 49:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2998s); [A message to my competitors 9:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t30Xo5uFkGk&t=563s))
19. A bigger team gives comparison data. With about 7 reps he couldn't tell good from bad; larger teams show whether one rep is in a rut or the whole market dipped. To test a change (e.g. a single offer), have one rep or part of the month use it and compare revenue per lead. ([Made $3M in one month 5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uci8AF7uU9A&t=340s); [1.8% close rate 11:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=683s); [Harsh business lessons 4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=246s))

**Culture and quality control**
20. Radical transparency and competition: every rep sees everyone's ~20 stats, and the bottom performers are let go monthly (visible weeks ahead). "My job is not to manage people but to create conditions where they succeed or leave." Deliberately overstaff so new reps keep replacing the weakest. Show each rep's month-over-month revenue-per-lead progress, which helps retention. Mid-month commission projections let reps choose to take time off or open weekends. ([Raw conversation $5m/mo 1:14:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4464s); [I failed 19:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=1150s); [$5M/mo sales system 10:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=652s); [Every number I tracked 12:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=770s))
21. A wins channel keeps closers' conviction up. With about 90% of the day spent hearing no, seeing results from clients they sold two weeks earlier lets them tell the next prospect they're losing money by not signing. ([Case studies make me millions 7:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=bxtXMHvdArs&t=434s))
22. Guard against overselling: reps don't sell to anyone who isn't qualified or ready to start. Wrongly closed clients are refunded with no commission (one repeat offender was made a public example), and there are fines for too many back-outs in the 7-day window. ([Funnel made me $3M 10:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=624s); [Explaining the $1m/mo offer 17:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=1020s); [Sales hiring playbook 9:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=586s))
23. Pick tools that fit the sales process. A $100k, 6-month HubSpot migration was hated by reps and rolled back within 14 hours to Sheets plus GHL, saving $10k/month in software and an estimated $1M+/month in deals lost to slowness. Ask every new client how they found you. Have marketing listen to sales calls; their best copywriter came from the sales team. ([Harsh business lessons 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=0s); [Went broke 19:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1154s); [Business With My Wife Q&A 2:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_j_83cglBA&t=158s))

> "The baseline metric that we make all of our decisions off of is revenue per lead." ([3:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=217s))
> "The leads suck argument is absolutely not true." ([2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=154s))
> "If I have a 30% or 40% close rate, this rather indicates that the price is too low" ([47:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2876s))

**For G&L:**
- Build the per-rep sheet from day one in Google Sheets/GHL (not a heavy CRM migration). Track leads allocated, show %, show-to-qualified %, qualified close %, revenue per lead, cash collected within 30 days, refunds, effective hourly rate and operational drag, with separate tiers for buyer-side and seller-side leads.
- Make **cash collected per lead** (within 30 days) the leaderboard metric. Advisory deals with deposits and success fees can distort "revenue", so pay and rank on cash actually collected, and track success fees separately as LTV.
- Tier leads from application data: e.g. tier 1 = sellers with $1M+ EBITDA or buyers with verified liquidity/an operating background; tier 3 = first-time buyers with under 1 year of planning or "just researching". New reps start on tier 3.
- Benchmark sanity checks: expect about 10–15% show-to-close. If G&L closes 30%+, raise the fee; if it drops near 5%, watch rep morale and wins per week.
- Weekly 30-minute huddle run by a player-coach lead, plus a #wins channel posting client milestones (LOI signed, vendor note agreed, business sold) so reps carry conviction into calls with sceptical 55+ owners.
- Plan rep headcount with a 20–30% buffer above the revenue target. Most 55+ sellers will slow down around holidays and end of financial year, so expect more timing and authority objections then.
- Coach by exception: red-flag any rep well below tier average and have them shadow the top rep's recordings. Give one fix per review. Record every call for compliance, which matters especially in regulated financial and M&A conversations.
- Strict no-oversell rule: no commission on refunds, and no selling to buyers without the minimum liquidity or experience, or to sellers whose businesses can't realistically be sold. Bad-fit clients hurt G&L's reputation with brokers and lenders.

---

<a id="e-numbers"></a>

## E. Numbers, Money & Scale: KPIs, Finance, Profit, Scaling Stages, Tools

### E1. KPIs & every number to track

**Rules**

1. **Revenue per lead (RPL) is the master KPI.** RPL = ticket price × close %. Sort the sales leaderboard by it, because close rate can be measured too many ways, is easy to game and means nothing without price and lead context (a Whole Foods cashier closes 99%). RPL beats revenue per call because it also shows whether the rep follows up and gets allocated leads onto calls, and you pay for every lead, not just every call. ([$5M sales system 3:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=186s), [1.8% close rate 5:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=310s), [Every number I tracked 3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=185s))
2. **Pull the revenue levers in this order: ticket price > number of leads > close rate.** Lead volume is the easiest lever to move, since you can double leads with ad budget but can't double close rate overnight. ([1.8% close rate 1:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=60s), [1.8% close rate 2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=155s))
3. **Optimise for revenue and ROAS, not for leading indicators** such as CTR, opt-ins, cost per lead, cost per booked call or shows. The cheapest calls are often non-buyers, and optimising for cheap leads nearly bankrupted him (~$500K of unsecured debt). A media buyer who doesn't own bottom-line revenue is doing you a disservice. The same ads perform very differently across clients because of downstream factors (sales skill, price, LTV, refunds). ([Ads $0–1M/mo 7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=433s), [$1M FB ROAS 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PVwA6gA633Y&t=0s), [$10K vs $1M ads 9:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2clMOGeFzJQ&t=553s))
4. **Track cost per SQL as the bridge metric.** An SQL is a lead the rep flags as showed and qualified, whether or not it closed. Cost per SQL = spend ÷ SQLs. There are roughly 3–5× more SQLs than sales, so SQLs give enough data to act on at low spend. An ad with 8 SQLs is more trustworthy than one with 2. On SQL-to-sale ratio, 4:1 is weak and 2:2 means every SQL bought. ([Ads for profit 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=61s), [Ads for profit 4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=245s), [Ads for profit 7:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=460s))
5. **Derive targets from your own LTV, not generic benchmarks.** For services, CAC should be ≤ 30% of LTV. Worked example: LTV $5,000 → CAC $1,500. At 20% close and 50% show, 2 clients need 20 booked calls, so the target is $150 per booked call. Then adjust for commissions, fees, refunds and rep differences. The two numbers that drive profit are CAC (sales + marketing) against LTV (fulfilment). ([2026 Meta Ads course 35:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2131s), [Lessons to $3M 37:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2237s))
6. **Track ad spend as a % of revenue.** When it creeps above 25%, pull back testing and reallocate to proven winners. ([$700K on FB ads 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=31s), [Ads $0–1M/mo 13:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=804s))
7. **Attribute every sale to its source**, down to organic vs paid, the platform, the ad and the specific video. Build identical but separate funnels per source (Meta, Google, IG organic, YouTube, email). Fixing attribution drove the $1M→$3M jump in 30 days. Tools range from Triple Whale or Hyros to Linktree or bit.ly links. ([Top 5 mistakes $1M 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XhqDVGpyXFw&t=30s), [$116K/day landing page 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=153s), [5 mistakes $30K/mo 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9o5csXRJXeU&t=92s))
8. **For short-cycle lead gen, use last-click attribution** (whatever booked the call most cheaply). His B2B cycle is ~5 days, and last click has been the most reliable signal across ~$10M of spend. Blended or "scientific" models are only for month-long enterprise cycles that have the spend to supply the data. ([$3M AMA 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=31s), [Ads for profit 7:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=430s))
9. **Measure closed revenue by source, not bookings.** At ~$500K/mo, organic gave 5–10% of leads but up to 60% of revenue, while paid gave 90% of leads, filled the calendar and closed poorly. Organic leads convert ~3× better than paid. ([Focus on revenue 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_oZlUE9Eygg&t=30s), [$116K/day landing page 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=153s))
10. **Keep a per-rep sheet with these columns:** leads allocated, calls booked, shows, call-to-show %, qualified, show-to-qualified %, deals won, qualified close %, show-to-close %, lead-to-close ratio, revenue per sale, total revenue, revenue per call, RPL, commission MTD, estimated monthly commission, effective hourly rate and operational cost. Add team and tier averages, and make all 20+ metrics visible to the whole team. ([Every number I tracked 1:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=93s), [$20K/mo rep 7:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=432s))
11. **Operational drag (efficiency cost) = (best rep's RPL in the tier − this rep's RPL) × this rep's leads.** Examples: a ~$1K/lead gap × 177 leads ≈ $150–185K/month lost, and one high-volume "top" rep cost ~$600K of drag. This makes moving reps up or down tiers objective. ([Every number I tracked 4:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=248s), [Every number I tracked 5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=340s), [$5M sales system 14:25](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=865s))
12. **Effective hourly rate = commission MTD ÷ shown calls** (each call counts as an hour), plus a run-rate projection of monthly commission. It shows reps what each extra call is worth and is a strong recruiting tool. ([$5M sales system 11:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=714s), [Every number I tracked 13:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=800s))
13. **Close rate, total revenue and revenue per call are "kindergarten" metrics.** Track 10–20 data points per rep, plus lost calls by objection type (budget, authority, need, timing) per rep and team-wide. ([Made $3M in a month 5:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uci8AF7uU9A&t=340s), [$20K/mo rep 8:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=494s))
14. **Put a dollar value on every application answer** (revenue per call by answer) and route on it. Measured differences: revenue bracket 643%, business vs free email ~250%, area code 278%, time in business 248%, booking lead time 737%. ([8 questions 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=31s), [8 questions 6:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=402s), [8 questions 15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=959s))
15. **Judge each ad or post against its funnel-stage job.** Top-of-funnel is judged on CTR and views, bottom-of-funnel on cost per call and ROAS. A video with 10× the views can drive a fraction of the sales. Keep some lower-ROAS ads running when ROAS isn't their job. ([$36K 1-1 training 1:04:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hp328yUV6O8&t=3847s), [Scaling to $5M raw 1:05:42](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3942s), [9-figure sales teams 51:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=3088s))
16. **Review ads on a fixed cadence.** Weekly, run all-time and 7-day lookbacks for housekeeping (kill losers, promote winners). Daily, run a rolling 3-day lookback; never judge on a single day. One day is too noisy for lead gen, and 7 days is too slow to catch a $1K/day problem. ([Managing $1M adspend 12:29](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=749s), [2026 Meta Ads course 42:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2533s))
17. **Forecast from unit economics.** Marketing spend per rep ÷ CPL = leads, and leads × that rep's RPL = forecast revenue. Cross-check with several models (RPL, revenue per call, calendar availability and others). Five models converged on $5.1–5.3M, which they hit. Predicting outcomes with numbers, then comparing the prediction to the result, is the top operator skill. ([Every number I tracked 11:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=708s), [$5M with wife 27:26](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=feWJhxN9M5g&t=1646s), [$3M month Q&A 9:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=556s))
18. **Set KPIs per role, and set them before hiring.** Sales: total revenue, RPL, refunds, LTV per sale. Fulfilment: refunds, support tickets, client results, client-communication frequency (a leading indicator). Cost centres get measured too: legal is tracked on open cases, resolved cases, money recovered and response time, and a recruiter on critical errors. Never let a senior hire define their own KPIs. ([5 lessons 1:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=92s), [Lessons to $3M 59:10](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=3550s), [Harsh lessons 1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=91s))
19. **Use these setter and dialer metrics:** hours online (6–7 quality hours/day), contact rate, unique dial rate and speed-to-lead (contacting within 5 minutes made a sale 100× more likely in a HubSpot/MIT study). Watch calls ended under 15 seconds (the opener needs work) and under 2 minutes (moving from opener to problem). ([9-figure sales teams 13:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=833s), [9-figure sales teams 17:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1051s))
20. **Build a show-up heat map by hour** from a 2-second post-call form. One 2-hour block may run at 10% show-up against 80% elsewhere, so move team training into the dead block. ([9-figure sales teams 25:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1548s))
21. **Measure client satisfaction once volume allows.** Past ~$100K/month, run NPS on the whole base every 30 days with standardised collection, because satisfaction caps price. ([1.8% close rate 10:23](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=623s))
22. **Expect seasonality.** Nov–Dec is rarely a growth window for B2B and professional services. Moving up-market changes seasonal behaviour, so last year's data may not transfer. ([I failed 11:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=682s), [I failed 9:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=590s))
23. **Measure content and channels by the bank balance, not vanity numbers.** His views fell while income rose ~3×. ([Why no views 8:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LjswhfXCmCo&t=520s))

**Benchmark table** (Daniel's own numbers unless noted; derive your own targets from LTV)

| Metric | Benchmark / target | Source |
|---|---|---|
| CAC vs LTV | CAC ≤ 30% of LTV (service rule of thumb) | [2026 Meta Ads course 35:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2131s) |
| Ad spend as % of revenue | **Differs by period:** 15% in the $3M month ([Made $3M in a month 18:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uci8AF7uU9A&t=1115s)); 24% in August, $700K → ~$3M ([$700K on FB ads 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=31s)); ~20% ([Scaling to $5M raw 54:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=3241s)). Ceiling rule: >25% means pull back testing ([Ads $0–1M/mo 13:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=804s)). Competitors at $5–10M/mo run 30–40% | as cited |
| ROAS | Daniel ~4× (24% spend); $1M+/mo spend → $5M+ ad revenue ([Ads for profit 1:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=61s)); "$1M in ads → ~$4M new business" ([Buy customers 9:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-sZaKSREzM4&t=558s)). Guest's thresholds: 4× "printing money", 3× goal, 2.5× OK, 2× relying on back end, <2× "stealing from the back end" ([Scaling to $10M 9:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=581s)) | as cited |
| Per-ad budget moves by ROAS | 9× ROAS: $3K→$4K/day; 5×: $2K→$1.5K; 2.7×: $1K→$500 or off, even with cheap calls | [Ads for profit 5:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o8qNXh62axk&t=307s) |
| Cost per booked call | **Changed over time:** ~$50 at the start vs ~$450 now ([Lessons to $3M 35:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2115s)); $500 account average ([Managing $1M adspend 7:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=465s)); test control ~$450 per *qualified* call, winners $239 ([Employees made an ad 3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fFmGhsryRiI&t=185s)) | as cited |
| Cost per qualified show | ~$1,500 now (vs ~$100 per qualified lead at the start); ~15% of those close | [Lessons to $3M 35:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=2115s) |
| Monthly funnel at >$1M/mo spend | 2,671 leads → ~39% DQ'd → 1,633 live calls → ~48% show (~800 shown) | [$5M sales system 0:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=31s) |
| Show rate | Team 48%, top rep 84% via pre-call outreach ([$5M sales system 2:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=153s)); passive reps on tier-3 ~30% ([Sales onboarding 1:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MBj6qrUx9W8&t=91s)); ~40% no-show ([1.8% close rate 8:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=530s)); direct-to-cart buyers 80–90% ([Scaling to $10M 13:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=795s)) | as cited |
| Pre-call engagement → shows & qualifies | No email open + no text reply ≈ 3%; one channel ≈ 14–16%; opened + replied ≈ 42% | [$116K/day landing page 15:59](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=959s), [100K sales calls 3:36](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MPhvqxVGzkw&t=216s) |
| Close rate (by definition) | 17% qualified-show→close; 12% show→close; 8% confirmed call→close; 6.6% call→close; **1.8% lead→close** | [1.8% close rate 8:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=530s), [$3M sales script 30:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eMrkPt0gqFM&t=1828s) |
| Close-rate sanity checks | 12% in a $3.5M month, so don't plan on 20–30% ([Won't make 1M/mo 3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=185s)); 30–40% means price too low ([Scaling to $5M raw 46:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2815s)); 60% means double prices ([1.8% close rate 6:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OAtocDg2Mqo&t=374s)); organic calls ~40% vs paid <10% ([Lessons to $3M 24:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=1471s)) | as cited |
| Close rate by segment | <$10K/mo revenue: 53% show, 43% qualified, 3% call→close (cut); higher brackets 10–12%; historic spend $5–50K/mo 11–14% | [8 questions 4:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=277s), [8 questions 9:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=556s) |
| Revenue per lead | Tier-3 leads ~$1,000/lead ([$20K/mo rep 5:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=308s)); routing best leads to best reps took RPL from $500 to $1,000 ([$3M funnel 3:38](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=218s)); removing the cheaper offer added +40% ([Harsh lessons 3:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XPfyJpWA6A&t=183s)) | as cited |
| Lead-to-close ratio | Best rep 10:1 vs another 25:1 | [Every number I tracked 10:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=646s) |
| Revenue per rep per month | Targets $1.2–1.5M; top reps $1.2M, $852K (part-time), $800K…; under $500K/mo = underperforming; $1M from ~200 calls | [Every number I tracked 11:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jgkuFYLRLnA&t=677s), [Every revenue level 11:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=714s), [Close $1M/mo 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxoxvG4bj90&t=0s) |
| Effective hourly rate (top reps) | ~$400–575/hour | [$5M sales system 11:54](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=714s) |
| Sales comp as % of revenue | Just over 5% all-in (~half the industry) ([100-person team 7:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5YaXZkjo0V0&t=467s)); ~6% ([$3M/mo team 4:06](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ds1rAvgIzrA&t=246s)); guest 20–25% of net cash + ~20% overhead ([Scaling to $10M 7:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=428s)) | as cited |
| Refunds | 7-day refunds ~20% → 10% → 8.8% over 6 months ([$20K/mo rep 11:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oLR7G2L6bDg&t=678s)); trial refunds ~40% → ~5% after an onboarding fix ([Made $5M in a month 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=215s)) | as cited |
| Cash collected in first 30 days | Benchmark ~30% of contract value; Daniel ~85% | [Scaling to $10M 1:24:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=5080s) |
| Missed payments | 40–60% on year-long high-ticket contracts | [Guarantees 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OsktcdNDATU&t=215s) |
| Churn | Inner circle 3–4% annualised (cap ~250 seats) | [$1M/mo offer 28:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AfvS5Tkhbis&t=1730s) |
| Delivery cost % of gross | <10% at $1M/mo; ≤5% at a few $M/mo; 0.5–1% for the largest firms | [Won't make 1M/mo 7:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=461s) |
| Revenue by source | Organic 5–10% of leads, up to 60% of revenue ([Focus on revenue 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_oZlUE9Eygg&t=30s)); later ads ~80% of sales volume, organic 20–40% of revenue, referrals <1%, outbound <0.01% ([$3M funnel 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=o1i_mFCOJZI&t=0s)); email 2–3% ([5 lessons 0:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=0s)) or <5% ([Scaling to $5M raw 1:07:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=4034s)) | as cited |
| Dialer metrics | Answer rate 20–25% on auto-dialers vs ~45% compliant; unique dial rate 30–40% ideal (10–15% means you need more leads); contact rate 60%+ dialing, ~80% with SMS/email, best seen 92% | [9-figure sales teams 7:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=433s), [10:48](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=648s), [12:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=740s) |
| Setter cadence | ~3 calls/day, max ~10/week per lead; triage calls 7–10 min | [9-figure sales teams 13:53](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=833s), [17:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1051s) |
| Closer capacity | Best at 70–80% capacity; cap at 5 calls in a row (calls 6–8 closed ~2%) | [9-figure sales teams 23:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1425s), [24:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JEUmEI4REu0&t=1455s) |
| Booking lead time | 2–7 days out ([$116K/day landing page 13:24](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=804s)); calls booked 1–6 h out ≈ $49 revenue per call, 6–72 h+ much better ([8 questions 15:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=928s)) | as cited |
| Creative volume | ≥1 good video per $5K of spend ([$700K on FB ads 1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7u3gnQT-IDo&t=62s)); ≥100 creatives before launching; ~800 live ads at $40K/day, ~1,000 at $50K/day ([$3M AMA 7:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=438s)) | as cited |
| Test budget per ad | 3–5× target KPI (e.g. $450–750 at $150/call); kill at >$1,000 spent with 0 or 1 result | [2026 Meta Ads course 40:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=2441s), [Managing $1M adspend 13:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJWuC8HG4Ps&t=780s) |
| Spend split | 80% proven / 20% testing ([$3M AMA 8:50](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rO6lGSXn42E&t=530s)); 20–50% for experiments ([Lessons to $3M 11:16](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=676s)) | as cited |
| Meta spend context | >$10K/mo = top 25% of Meta spenders (Databox) | [Ads $0–1M/mo 1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=62s) |

> "The baseline metric that we make all of our decisions off of is revenue per lead." ([$5M sales system 3:37](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MCPvsVEAvoQ&t=217s))

> "Going from 12% to 24% close rate is the same as dropping your ad costs by 50%." ([Won't make 1M/mo 5:39](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=339s))

> "It doesn't matter what anyone says. Don't even listen to me. Measure your own data." ([8 questions 17:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LkCQwWRMlIs&t=1020s))

**For G&L:**
- Build the G&L scorecard around **revenue per lead, split by lead tier**: seller with $1M+ EBITDA, seller under $1M, funded buyer, unfunded buyer. Sort the closer leaderboard by RPL and compute operational drag each month.
- Set G&L's cost-per-booked-call and cost-per-SQL targets from **LTV (retainer + success fee) × 30%**, not from other niches. A seller engagement worth tens of thousands justifies a high cost per call.
- Define an SQL for G&L, e.g. a seller with verified EBITDA and an exit timeline under 24 months, or a buyer with liquid capital and operating experience. Fire the Meta conversion only on SQLs.
- Run separate, identical funnels (and UTMs) per source: Meta, YouTube, LinkedIn/organic, referrals/partners like accountants and brokers. Report closed revenue and cash by source monthly.
- Because G&L's sales cycle is longer than Daniel's ~5 days, track lead→SQL weekly but judge ads on 30–90-day revenue cohorts. Watch cash-collected-in-30-days against the ~30% benchmark.
- Put a dollar value on every application answer (EBITDA band, owner age, timeline, liquid capital, business email domain) and route on it.
- **For clients:** give sell-side owners a KPI dashboard (revenue per customer, gross margin, CAC:LTV, churn, revenue concentration). Buyers' due diligence and PE look at these first, and clean, tracked numbers shorten diligence.

### E2. Finance, cash flow & debt

**Rules**

1. **Use a fixed reinvestment split in growth months.** Of ~$2M collected, ~50% went back into ads. Of the remaining ~$1M, half went to existing staff, a quarter to new hires and a quarter to one-offs (flying people in, training). This took them from $1.4M to $3M. ([$3M month Q&A 2:03](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YkEuo4P6vKs&t=123s))
2. **Take ~10% of topline as profit and reinvest the rest.** It is equally easy at $100K, $500K or $5M/month. He took essentially nothing for two years, and for nearly three years almost every dollar went back into testing. Keep a small safety net, but reinvest for maximum upside. ([Every revenue level 19:41](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=1181s), [Scared of broke 3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=184s), [Scared of broke 4:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XJZf_eMSfSo&t=245s))
3. **Build a cash reserve before big ad-spend risks.** The $1.5M→$3M jump waited only until savings were on the sidelines. He ran a capped side business on autopilot and used its profits as reserves for the new company. ([Why I disappeared 11:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KeU8LhMBdbA&t=706s), [Buy customers 7:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-sZaKSREzM4&t=465s))
4. **Don't fund losses with unsecured debt, and keep liquidity.** While losing $10–20K a day he ran up ~$500K of uncollateralised credit-card debt (Amex, Chase, Capital One, BofA) and was down to $400 in the bank. Illiquid assets didn't help: a plane, a Porsche, and home equity that would take 30 days to refinance and was lowballed. ([Why I disappeared 4:31](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KeU8LhMBdbA&t=271s), [Went broke 14:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=870s), [5 lessons 6:09](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J0YiARB-zXY&t=369s))
5. **Watch CAC and cash collected, not topline.** Revenue grew $120K → $350K → $500K → ~$700K while CAC was ~150% of topline, with large receivables and no idea which clients would pay. Scaling ads from ~$50K to $500K+/mo at ~$700K topline nearly bankrupted him in 3 months, because blended reporting hid that ~60% of revenue was organic. ([Went broke 7:46](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=466s), [Went broke 8:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=497s), [$116K/day landing page 3:04](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w5bYv2CBDZE&t=184s))
6. **Be cash-on-cash profitable every 30 days.** On a fast-changing offer you never get clean LTV data; at best, estimate it from weekly-cohort survivorship. ([Won't make 1M/mo 0:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Y8WB72fKX1Q&t=30s))
7. **Expect "recover CAC on the front end" to break around $1M/mo of spend.** Beyond that point you must break even or lose on the front end and fund ads from receivables, upsells and continuity, so know your true LTV beyond 30 days. ([Ads $0–1M/mo 17:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qyBmpjamXWU&t=1052s))
8. **Collect as much as possible upfront.** Upfront billing means the client covers acquisition and onboarding in month 1–2 instead of you waiting 5–6 months. SaaS example: $100/mo × 24 months = $2,400 LTV justifies $1,000 CAC but takes ~10 months to recoup, which stalls ad scaling without VC money. Low-ticket MRR as a core model almost needs VC; higher-priced B2B MRR with setup fees can be bootstrapped. ([Paid 20K lesson 6:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KkLU1WjvZxM&t=365s), [Paid 20K lesson 3:33](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KkLU1WjvZxM&t=213s), [Iman Gadzhi call 7:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ocPw7KvY710&t=434s))
9. **Moving from MRR to upfront package pricing doubled the business** ($1.3M → $3M/mo in 30 days). Weekly ~$1,500 charges landing before visible results caused rage and refunds. Collect the most upfront and put the remainder on an instalment plan. ([Focus on revenue 2:01](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_oZlUE9Eygg&t=121s), [Every revenue level 26:58](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=1618s), [Made $5M in a month 2:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jm14bVak6rA&t=154s))
10. **Set contract length from the churn of happy clients.** Average ~5 months → 6-month minimum. Make monthly unattractive and discount 3-, 6- and 12-month prepayment. Worked example: an exit-planning consultant at $6K/month whose clients left at month 5 moved to a 6-month minimum, $36K monthly or $30K paid in full. ([Scaling to $5M raw 37:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CLJ9AnFuSZk&t=2233s), [Every revenue level 30:34](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AmXrrw8OGa0&t=1834s))
11. **Use the collections benchmark and its price rule.** ~30% of total contract value collected in the first 30 days is typical; Daniel collects ~85%. If you collect too much upfront, raise the price. ([Scaling to $10M 1:24:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=5080s))
12. **Match the financing term to the programme term.** 24-month loans on 12-month programmes meant ~50% of monthly cash came from loans that clients kept paying after delivery ended, which killed renewals and likely got them dropped by Affirm. Matching terms cut financing to 20–30%, then 10% of cash. ([Scaling to $10M 1:09:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4195s))
13. **Price financing so net cash is equal.** $10K by card at 3% nets $9,700; financing at 15% nets $8,500, so the financed price must be higher. Internal plans cost more in total (2 × $4,100 = $8,200 vs $7,000 paid in full), with a quarterly minimum to cover onboarding. Splitit uses the buyer's card limit with 12 months interest-free; Klarna-type options can cost 20–30%. "Deferred PIF" lets a plan buyer upgrade to paid-in-full within 30 days. ([Scaling to $10M 1:12:28](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4348s), [Scaling to $10M 1:19:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=4775s), [Scaling to $10M 1:26:12](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=5172s))
14. **Track receivables actively.** Hold payment status in a database (delinquent, approved vs unapproved pause, ghosting) visible to account managers. Make "payments on time" a guarantee condition, since missed payments run 40–60% on year-long contracts. ([64 tools 3:05](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=185s), [Guarantees 3:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OsktcdNDATU&t=215s))
15. **Manage payment-processor risk.** Stripe flagged them for voluntary refunds (despite low disputes) and held a percentage of revenue for ~3 months. They moved via Zoho to HubSpot payments and negotiated a better rate at multi-million volume. Keep two payment methods on autopay for the ad account. ([64 tools 13:52](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXQ0KLtz1kA&t=832s), [2026 Meta Ads course 54:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eEiyrvM1nB8&t=3280s))
16. **Model the P&L in modules.** Fulfilment is roughly fixed, sales is a fixed %, and marketing swings. When reporting profit, reserve 15–20% of cash for future delivery owed. The guest's net profit was 45% of cash. ([Scaling to $10M 18:21](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r6KhTStpbZc&t=1101s))
17. **Plan cash around the deal cycle.** With a ~30-day cycle only ~half of leads close inside the month, so double the leads (1,000 instead of 500) to hit month one. ([$0–500K in 90 days 1:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AZC82IKCiVI&t=62s))
18. **Plan big pushes months ahead and set a risk budget.** A sudden push from $800K to $1.1M of spend left sales flat and ~$300K vanished. Founders at $2–4M/mo turn risk-averse, so set an explicit stop-loss style risk budget. ([I failed 2:35](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aUgSDt1Kp4g&t=155s), [Lessons to $3M 9:13](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kkqcPEj0-po&t=553s))
19. **Don't build fixed overhead ahead of proven acquisition.** $700K topline with ~$200K/month payroll left razor-thin margins. Commission-based acquisition and fulfilment roles let the team survive lean months. ([Went broke 12:56](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=776s), [Went broke 23:22](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1402s))
20. **There's a floor on cost-cutting and no ceiling on income,** so put your energy into controlling acquisition. ([Went broke 30:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XwERThzQQBU&t=1807s))
21. **Budget for learning losses.** Year one showed zero net profit on several million of spend. He started with five years' experience and ~$1M he was prepared to lose, then went $500K further into debt.
